14:T3517,# [cloud][billing] Define the server-owned Plus and Pro subscription catalog and public contracts

## Summary

Introduce the only authoritative recurring-plan catalog for Plus at $30/month and Pro at $100/month. Keep all prices, allowances, rate envelopes, resource ceilings, eligibility rules, and Stripe bindings server-owned and fail closed on provider drift.

## Current state

The Cloud stack has canonical Stripe-customer authority, durable one-time credit checkout, a six-decimal purchased-credit ledger, auto-top-up, invoices, rate-limit snapshots, and Billing/Settings UI. Checkout is `mode: payment`; there is no recurring catalog, subscription DTO, entitlement projection, Customer Portal, or subscription-event processing. Existing paid/growth inference tiers are derived from cumulative credit grants and must not become the subscription authority.

## Product contract

- `plus_monthly`: $30 USD monthly, $25 expiring allowance, RPM 120/200/60/10.
- `pro_monthly`: $100 USD monthly, $90 expiring allowance, RPM 300/600/120/30.
- No trial, coupon, annual, or client-supplied plan in v1.
- Recommended resource ceilings: Plus 100 cloud characters, 100 sandboxes, 25 containers, 25 GiB; Pro 500, 500, 100, 100 GiB; apps remain 25.

## Implementation

- Add an immutable, versioned TypeScript catalog in Cloud shared services.
- Define typed plan key, interval, currency, amount, allowance, funding classes, four RPM fields, and resource ceilings.
- Bind Stripe prices using validated server environment variables only.
- On provider lookup, verify active recurring monthly USD price, exact amount, approved product, and deployment/test-live consistency.
- Add public plan and subscription DTOs to the canonical Cloud API types; never expose Stripe price/product IDs.
- Add `GET /api/v1/subscriptions/plans` as a thin projection of the server catalog.
- Add startup/deploy configuration validation and explicit unavailable errors.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] Exactly two active v1 plans exist with stable keys and catalog version.
- [ ] Clients cannot submit or override amount, currency, allowance, limits, or Stripe IDs.
- [ ] Missing or malformed bindings fail closed before checkout or entitlement publication.
- [ ] Price, product, currency, recurrence, activity, or livemode drift is rejected and alerted.
- [ ] Public DTOs contain no provider secrets or provider identifiers.
- [ ] UI and later services consume DTO/catalog values rather than duplicating plan math.

## Tests and evidence

- Exact catalog snapshots, duplicate-key rejection, malformed configuration, provider drift, test/live mismatch, and DTO secret scans.
- Route authorization/caching/error-state tests.
- Real Stripe test-mode preflight showing both approved recurring prices and rejection of a deliberately wrong fixture price.

## Ownership and dependencies

Cloud shared billing owns the catalog and DTOs; Cloud API owns the read route; deployment owners provision bindings. This is the first subscription issue and blocks persistence, checkout, entitlements, and UI.

Current immutable v1 catalog is $30/$100 with null resource ceilings; add a deliberate product/catalog revision preserving historical records and verified server-owned Stripe prices. Integrate #30638 generic app contracts rather than adding a second authority. Decide included Dedicated resources, trial allowance/start trigger, legacy migration and tax presentation before launch. Acceptance: both amounts/intervals and approved entitlements agree across quote, Checkout and Billing; price drift rejects; old records remain reconstructable; staging #30565 preflight plus sandbox object evidence. Source: https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/06963a19b01c3720aedcf77c77434936e8654270/packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/services/subscription-catalog.ts#L95

## Critical implementation audit — 2026-09-06

Source: `8fea4f94c29c7712ce3caf303e3350150622d048`. Source/live GitHub-state review only; no provider, database, deployment or payment mutation and no new billing acceptance run.

### Existing authority and remaining contract

The original “no recurring catalog” description is historical: #23154 and trailing-slash CORS #24989 are merged. [The current catalog](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/8fea4f94c29c7712ce3caf303e3350150622d048/packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/services/subscription-catalog.ts#L18) is deliberately immutable v1, $30/$100, with $25/$90 allowances and null resource ceilings. It validates provider amount/currency/product/recurrence/mode and rejects price-level trial metadata. [The public route](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/8fea4f94c29c7712ce3caf303e3350150622d048/packages/cloud/api/v1/subscriptions/plans/route.ts#L27) publishes only after provider preflight, with explicit 503/no-store on failure. Existing operational comments diagnose missing bindings at historical deployments; they do not prove today's deployment state.

The Sprint 3 request is a **new $29/$99 Dedicated revision with card-required seven-day first trial**, not permission to mutate historical v1 amounts in place. Generic app/subscriber/trial ownership is #30638; current org catalog cannot silently become a second app billing authority. #22930 remains the decision dependency for unresolved policy; #30565 owns fresh staging preflight. Recommended historical resource ceilings are not ratified limits.

### Implementation sequence

1. Record a decision table for new revision identity, account/app subscriber scope, first-trial eligibility identity, trial start trigger, trial funding, included Dedicated resources, paid allowances, legacy migration and tax presentation. The requested prices/duration/card requirement are settled; other values must remain explicitly unresolved until owners decide. Preserve Stan's catalog ownership and Shaw's integration role.
2. Add a new immutable revision and historical resolver rather than editing v1. Update strict schemas and public SDK DTOs deliberately; catalogVersion is currently constrained to one literal. Model active purchasable revisions separately from resolvable historical revisions and define old-price servicing without exposing Stripe identifiers publicly. Existing billing revisions must still reconstruct their original amount/allowance.
3. Bind new approved test/live recurring prices on the server. Preserve exact validation of mode, currency, amount, interval, product/activity and quantity semantics; invalidate verified cache on binding/revision change. Keep trial eligibility/start in generic lifecycle authority, not in client parameters or ad hoc price metadata. Review the current price-level-trial rejection intentionally: [Stripe Checkout trials](https://docs.stripe.com/payments/checkout/free-trials?payment-ui=stripe-hosted) can be configured on the subscription data while collecting the future payment method. A seven-day trial does not require changing legacy price objects.
4. Project one authoritative quote/catalog DTO into #30638 checkout and Billing consumers, with revision, availability and supported trial offer. Do not duplicate allowance/price/resource math in UI. Reject unsupported combinations before checkout rather than publishing guessed entitlements.
5. Test historic v1 lookup and persisted-record replay alongside new $29/$99 quotes; exercise mismatched currency/amount/product/recurrence/mode and stale binding caches through actual provider adapter/route boundaries. Preserve slash/no-slash route and CORS authorization coverage. Avoid catalog-literal snapshots as the sole proof.
6. Complete #30565 preflight with approved real sandbox objects, verified deployment SHA, provider-neutral public readback and protected provider-object evidence. Compare quote, hosted Checkout and Billing amounts/revision/trial disclosure. Provisioning belongs to its owner; this catalog issue neither grants a trial nor starts a resource. Run shared/API/SDK tests, typecheck/lint, route/SDK generation audits and root verify.

Acceptance boundary: the 1.5-day slice is the revision/contract integration after policy and generic authority are available. Operational preflight, entitlement rollout and checkout lifecycle remain separate dependencies; keep the issue open when only local code passes. No secret values or real-money evidence belongs in the issue.

<!-- guardian-project19-adversarial-2026-09-06:start -->
Independent adversarial review at `b676280384b15df54204276e34bb8740db26936f`. Live issue read; this supplements the earlier plan. Source-proven means the checked-in logic establishes the observation, not that a deployed provider incident was reproduced. No provider/infra mutations or dependency installation.

### P1 — Persist the purchased catalog revision in the durable command, not just the plan name

The proposed immutable v2 catalog is necessary but insufficient. Durable checkout commands store target_plan_key, request_digest and provider_idempotency_key, with no target catalog revision, immutable price binding, or retrievable quote payload. applyCheckoutResult matches a lifecycle revision by plan key and provider digest, not catalog identity. A digest detects disagreement only when the original full input is available; it cannot reconstruct a price after a worker restart.

Concrete integration counterexample: enqueue Plus under v1; deploy v2 before recovery; recovery has only plus_monthly and now resolves its current binding. Reusing the provider idempotency key with changed request parameters may fail; issuing a new key to work around that risks a second purchase. This is a source-proven missing persistence field and a prospective worker failure sequence; no production checkout worker using it was found.

Amendment: add immutable target catalog version plus validated provider environment/account/price identity and the complete replayable command payload or its canonical durable reference. Include every field in idempotency equality and checkout-result authority matching. Store explicit trial/tax/quantity policy identity where it changes provider parameters. Historical command recovery must use its original approved revision; availability of a new active catalog must not retarget it. Test deployment between PREPARED, provider dispatch, uncertain outcome and reconciliation, including a retired-but-historically-valid price and an explicitly unavailable historical binding.

Sources: [command persistence](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/b676280384b15df54204276e34bb8740db26936f/packages/cloud/shared/src/db/schemas/subscription-billing-operations.ts#L53), [replay comparison](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/b676280384b15df54204276e34bb8740db26936f/packages/cloud/shared/src/db/repositories/subscription-billing-operations.ts#L131), [result matching](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/b676280384b15df54204276e34bb8740db26936f/packages/cloud/shared/src/db/repositories/subscription-billing-operations.ts#L450).

### P2 — Separate the historical registry from the two active public offerings

Appending both versions to the existing array fails before dispatch: buildCatalog rejects duplicate plan keys and requires exactly two records. Merely changing CATALOG_VERSION instead removes v1 from resolveSubscriptionPlanDefinition and prevents old entitlement rebuilds. Introduce a registry keyed by (catalogVersion, planKey), with a separate active-public selection and versioned provider bindings. Exercise v1 replay and v2 publication in the same process, not separate fixtures which each contain only one version. [Source](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/b676280384b15df54204276e34bb8740db26936f/packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/services/subscription-catalog.ts#L65).

The SQL schema already accepts nonempty catalog versions; there is no evidence that a blanket rewrite of historical catalog strings is needed. Do not rewrite journal/allowance history. Free entitlement defaults are independently hardcoded in TypeScript and a database insert trigger; if free policy changes, migrate the trigger and fresh-org contract too, while preserving historical policy provenance. [Trigger](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/b676280384b15df54204276e34bb8740db26936f/packages/cloud/shared/src/db/migrations/0373_subscription_authority.sql#L67).

### Research limits / attempts to disprove

The verification cache already keys on credentials, environment and price/product IDs, so the earlier general concern must not become an invented key-rotation defect. Provider-object mismatches are already checked. Current bootstrap source did not establish the old comment's catalog-blocks-entire-bootstrap claim. Existing derivation test was attempted with Bun 1.3.14: it could not load drizzle-orm (0 tests executed), so no database concurrency run is claimed.
<!-- guardian-project19-adversarial-2026-09-06:end -->
15:T145c,## Decision requested

Ratify what a Stripe refund or chargeback does to Cloud rights and actively billed resources, and when those rights may be restored.

### A. Immediate billing hold — recommended

Keep the existing idempotent proportional credit clawback, and place the organization in an explicit server-owned billing-hold state when the reversal makes its funded position invalid. New money spend and provisioning fail closed. Product must define below whether existing billable resources stop immediately or follow an explicit bounded window, and which read/export capabilities remain available. Restoration requires authoritative provider settlement plus a valid funded state; the client never infers it.

### B. Balance-only enforcement

Keep only the current credit clawback/shortfall record. The implementation floors the organization balance at zero, records any unrecovered amount, and disables auto-top-up, but there is no explicit cross-surface hold contract and already-running resources may continue accruing cost. This is simpler but leaves inconsistent behavior and unowned debt exposure across products.

### C. Manual review before restriction

Record the reversal and wait for an operator decision before changing entitlements. This reduces accidental suspension but leaves a known abuse window and requires an operator SLA, queue, audit trail, and safe default when the SLA expires.

## Exact policy fields required

Please select A, B, or C and define, without implementation guesses:

1. Trigger semantics for voluntary refunds, partial refunds, `charge.dispute.funds_withdrawn`, and `charge.dispute.funds_reinstated`.
2. Credit treatment: proportional clawback is currently implemented; the current schema forbids negative balances and records the unrecovered portion. Confirm whether that shortfall becomes authoritative debt, is written off, or triggers another state.
3. Immediate gates for inference, storage writes/reads, domain and other one-off purchases, API-key use, new apps, containers, shared agents, and dedicated agents.
4. Treatment and exact timing for already-running billable resources (continue, suspend, stop, or delete). If timing is not immediate, provide the exact duration and who pays during it.
5. Which non-billable capabilities remain available (sign-in, billing history/receipts, data read/export, cancellation/deletion).
6. Restoration rules after a won dispute or repayment, including whether restoration is automatic or reviewed.
7. UI state, notification, receipt, support/escalation, and audit requirements.
8. Interaction with future subscription states and dunning if #20328 proceeds with subscriptions.

## Current-state evidence

Revalidated on `develop` on 2026-08-22:

- `packages/cloud/api/src/queue/stripe-event.ts` handles `charge.refunded`, `charge.dispute.funds_withdrawn`, and `charge.dispute.funds_reinstated`.
- `packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/services/credits.ts` applies idempotent cumulative-delta clawbacks, floors `credit_balance` at zero under the organization lock, records `unrecovered_clawback_usd`, and disables auto-top-up.
- `charge.dispute.funds_reinstated` restores only the clawback amount that was actually applied; it does not resolve the unrecovered shortfall or establish a global restoration/hold authority.
- The merged payment-request receipt work provides provider-neutral settlement evidence, but it does not define a product-wide entitlement/resource hold.
- No open issue currently records the required cross-surface policy. Historical #10920/#10938 closed the missing Stripe clawback implementation, not this product decision.

## Scope and non-scope

This issue is a human product-decision record. It performs no payment, refund, provider call, deployment, database change, resource suspension, or production mutation. Do not implement a grace period, trial, plan entitlement, or deletion policy until the owner supplies it explicitly.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] A product owner records exactly `Decision: A`, `Decision: B`, or `Decision: C`.
- [ ] Every policy field above is answered or explicitly delegated to a linked product specification.
- [ ] Bounded implementation issues cover the server authority, per-route enforcement, active-resource reconciliation, UI/receipts, and adversarial tests.
- [ ] Tests include partial refund, refund after consumption, duplicate/replayed events, lost response, dispute withdrawn/reinstated, and concurrent spend versus hold activation.

## Claim / status

Human product decision required. Keep this issue human-gated until the policy is explicit; engineering claims begin only on the bounded implementation follow-ups.

16:T450f,# [views][interactions] Make every plugin operation chat/voice-native and keep full views as rich projections

## Summary

Move business operations behind typed use-cases/actions that can be invoked consistently through chat, voice, widgets/interactions, shortcuts, and full views. Views remain valuable rich projections for dense browsing and direct manipulation; they must not be the only path to product capability.

## Required architecture

- For every inventory operation, define canonical input/output/error/authorization/idempotency and owning service/use-case.
- Views become protocol adapters that call the same operation as agent actions and connector interaction callbacks.
- Add a presentation envelope describing compact card/widget, full view deep link, confirmation/approval, secure entry/OAuth, progress, receipt, and fallback semantics.
- Map the minimum portable vocabulary: approval, confirmation, setup/auth, choice/form, task/status, list/detail, and typed error/recovery.
- Use universal connector interactions for chat; secret/OAuth requests stay in secure link-out/native sensitive flows.
- Voice reads concise summaries, gathers safe structured fields, confirms side effects, and provides a visual continuation when dense/secret input is required.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] Every non-view-only operation is reachable through chat and, where semantically usable, voice.
- [ ] View/chat/voice/widget routes call the same use-case and return the same authorization/result reason codes.
- [ ] No view component performs unique provider/database business mutation.
- [ ] Side effects are idempotent and confirmations bind actor, operation, arguments, scope, and expiry.
- [ ] Unsupported connector UI downgrades semantically rather than hiding the operation.
- [ ] View-only classifications are rare, justified, and limited to genuinely dense/visual affordances.
- [ ] Secrets never transit ordinary chat/voice transcripts.

## Tests and real evidence

- Generated operation-coverage ledger, view/action/callback parity, direct-service bypass denial, confirmation replay/tamper/expiry, voice ambiguity/correction, secret redaction, and connector fallback.
- For every view, demonstrate at least one representative read and mutation through the view and chat/voice projection against real backing state. Record operation/audit IDs, trajectories, connector receipts, and UI evidence.

## Dependencies

Depends on authoritative view inventory, universal interactions, roles/capability grants, and domain-specific canonical operations.

## Current-source audit and implementation plan — 2026-09-06

Baseline: `8fea4f94c29c7712ce3caf303e3350150622d048`; source-backed review, not live acceptance. Preserve the distinction between this broad semantic-parity parent and its three-day Sprint 2 slice: contextual/mixed navigation in the same turn.

### Existing behavior and verified gaps

- [The current contextual evaluator](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/8fea4f94c29c7712ce3caf303e3350150622d048/plugins/plugin-app-control/src/evaluators/view-context.ts#L21-L80) uses a fixed eleven-view enum, a static mapping prompt and an English activity prefilter. Schema/parser also validate against this fixed list. Unlike #30682 exact alias routing, this path cannot naturally admit a newly registered contextual destination. The English prefilter can skip a non-English situation before the model sees it; multilingual failure needs a live reproduction, not an assumption about all languages.
- [Its processor](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/8fea4f94c29c7712ce3caf303e3350150622d048/plugins/plugin-app-control/src/evaluators/view-context.ts#L89-L135) checks live registration/current view and directly calls the loopback navigation client. Failed catalog reads become an undefined/no-op result; current-view read failures continue into navigation. It does not use the main VIEWS action/result receipt path, and its call carries no originating-client identity. These are concrete structural differences, not proof that every existing call navigates another client.
- [Registration and gating](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/8fea4f94c29c7712ce3caf303e3350150622d048/plugins/plugin-app-control/src/evaluators/view-context.ts#L136-L209) deliberately place this work after the response. The already-sent reply cannot describe the later navigation outcome. Existing tests use mocked loopback/model decisions; they do not prove same-turn grounding or real multi-operation execution.
- Exact navigation already has a pre-action evaluator and canonical VIEWS result path. [runViewsShow](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/8fea4f94c29c7712ce3caf303e3350150622d048/plugins/plugin-app-control/src/actions/views-show.ts#L582-L645) reads the live catalog and [delivery/result code](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/8fea4f94c29c7712ce3caf303e3350150622d048/plugins/plugin-app-control/src/actions/views-show.ts#L689-L713) carries caller-owned navigation. Preserve these owners and #29670's optimistic switch plus original request.
- #17028 is closed, and [current direct-action heuristics](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/8fea4f94c29c7712ce3caf303e3350150622d048/packages/core/src/services/message/direct-action-heuristics.ts#L1291-L1382) explicitly give owner-domain mutations/reads precedence over navigation. Do not weaken these rules when adding contextual selection. Calendar navigation cannot satisfy creating an event, and a Todos view cannot satisfy persisting a task.
- The broad issue's operation parity is still not proven: #24280 closed and its ledger PR #24343 closed unmerged. The existing #23110 generator discovers declarations/capability names, not universal per-control authorization/idempotency/projection coverage. Do not close this parent after completing the contextual-navigation slice.

### Detailed Sprint 2 implementation

1. **Reproduce with full real turns.** Capture current ordering for a contextual calendar request, a mixed explicit navigation plus event-draft request, and a dynamically registered contextual view. Record final-response time, action calls, navigation result, domain result and actual mounted view under one turn identity. This identifies whether a case is lost at prefilter, candidate selection, late evaluation or delivery.
2. **Define a typed in-turn navigation intent.** Reuse the canonical planning/action result protocol with explicit requested/optional visual continuation, candidate view, reason and no-navigation outcome. Use authorized live view metadata from #23110/#30682 as planner context; validate model selections against it at dispatch. Keep all message text, including late clauses, intact. Avoid a new fixed list, keyword-only contextual classifier or separate parallel navigation registry.
3. **Move selection before the final reply, execution through VIEWS.** Integrate contextual selection with the existing planner/response-handler seam; represent optional navigation alongside domain operations rather than forcing VIEWS as the sole candidate. Keep ordering dependencies explicit: navigation can precede relevant UI interaction, but server-backed domain work must not require a view to open. Remove the old post-response side-effect processor only when the in-turn owner covers its intended cases; do not run both and race. Retain genuine non-navigation post-turn evaluators.

Compound work stays with the planner; exact standalone shortcuts retain #30682. Explicit domain mutations and reads keep #17028 precedence, including its push-up cadence transcript. Resolve ambiguity before any affected side effect, and preserve independently authorized work when a visual continuation is unavailable. Consent/confirmation must bind actor, operation, arguments, scope and expiry wherever the domain requires it.
5. **Carry separate receipts into continuation.** Persist navigation disposition separately from event/task mutation or draft receipts. Feed actual results into the same turn's final model context. A successful switch is never an event-save receipt; a draft remains a draft. Missing view, denied navigation, failed delivery, unavailable provider and domain error need distinct outcomes. Use typed errors at boundaries instead of swallowing loopback failures as completed or silent success.
6. **Reuse acknowledgment and delivery infrastructure.** Coordinate #30681 so progress remains nonterminal and no reply claims a switch before its receipt. Preserve originating-client delivery and deduplication from VIEWS; check response ownership/cancellation after awaits. A canceled/superseded turn must not execute a delayed optional switch. No action-specific canned acknowledgment or global broadcast.

## Independent adversarial review #23111 — 2026-09-06

Source `b676280384b15df54204276e34bb8740db26936f`. Scrutinized contextual processor/eligibility, exact response-handler injection, and full VIEWS target selection before proposing in-turn integration.

## P1 — Moving contextual selection in-turn does not prevent whole-message intent from overwriting a later explicit tool target

[plugins/plugin-app-control/src/actions/views-show.ts:583](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/b676280384b15df54204276e34bb8740db26936f/plugins/plugin-app-control/src/actions/views-show.ts#L583) derives rigidIntentViewId from the complete original message, then resolveIntentView, separately extracts the model's target, and resolves it. Lines 633–650 subsequently replace the resolved target with registered whole-message intent whenever different. Thus a mixed request that legitimately plans successive different views can repeatedly resolve the original dominant view instead of the current tool's explicit target. Real matcher probe returned calendar for `Open calendar and create a meeting tomorrow`; the same static logic covers a later explicit notes target when the original message still matches calendar. No live two-navigation effect is claimed.

This contradicts any assumption that “send contextual selection through VIEWS” alone preserves typed per-step selection. The override intentionally corrects weak-model hallucinations for single-domain commands, so deleting it wholesale loses existing behavior.

Amendment: distinguish an exact standalone-command resolution from a planner-owned explicit per-step navigation target in the typed intent/result contract. Apply rigid correction only to the former; validate every latter target against authorized current registry/presentation state without re-resolving unrelated original clauses. Carry clause/step identity and explicit no-navigation constraints. Test two distinct sequential view targets, mixed domain+navigation, quoted view terms, dynamic contextual target competing with a built-in noun, and exact standalone correction. Assert target/order and complete domain effects.

## P1 — The pre-planner shortcut can erase compound routing before contextual integration sees it

The matcher actually matches quoted/metalinguistic and compound requests; the shortcut returns clearCandidateActions, clearParentActionHints and deterministicToolCall VIEWS. See #30682 independent probe. The planned new contextual path must not simply run after that earlier narrowing and assume original domain candidates survive. Amend dependency: one strict exact-command applicability decision shared with #30682 before the shortcut may clear hints; compound intent remains in normal planning with full original request.

## Contextual processor counterevidence and preservation decisions

The existing processor verifies live target registration and avoids navigating when already current (view-context.ts:110–120), so it is not blindly accepting arbitrary model IDs. However list-read failure returns undefined and current-view failure continues, as prior audit states. New typed unavailable outcomes should preserve the no-duplicate/already-current behavior and positive registration check while eliminating hidden post-response mutation. Do not remove it until replacement covers contextual eligibility and background service registration on all selected targets.

No new domain-semantic defect is asserted. Source and guides support same-turn separation of presentation and domain effect; only a real runtime/renderer test can show timing, actor scope and applied view. Full evaluator import probe was blocked by missing generated core keyword module; the shared matcher executed under pinned Bun without dependencies.

Further disconfirming caller checks: downstream routing can still terminate ignored/stopped/addressed-to-other turns (message.ts:9549–9580); direct-action rules have explicit candidate-replacement exceptions (message.ts:4910–4936); and deterministic dispatch rechecks role/context/private-action/arguments/account/validate through the canonical executor (message.ts:10265–10291). The later general candidate inference at 9691 is suppressed when candidateActionsClearedByEvaluators is true, but it is not the only gate. Therefore this review proves a wrong proposed deterministic navigation and a context-loss risk, not guaranteed final navigation or bypass of authorization. Full exact-turn integration is required to determine the final outcome for each probe.

17:T94e8,# [views][audit] Make the plugin-view inventory authoritative and fail on collisions or semantic coverage gaps

## Summary

Generate one authoritative inventory of every registered plugin/app view, route, slot, operation, backing action/API, chat/voice interaction, role/capability gate, responsive state, and test/evidence status. Fail CI on collisions, orphaned controls, or incomplete coverage lists.

## Current evidence

- The authoritative audit found 22 GUI views: views-manager, focus, calendar, computer-use-sessions, contacts, documents, cloud, finances, goals, health, inbox, messages, notes, phone, relationships, lifeops-live-test, task-coordinator, orchestrator, cockpit, todos, trajectory-logger, and wallet.
- Built-in Documents and plugin Documents collide on route/ID; registration order can hide the plugin surface.
- Existing tests/manifests enumerate inconsistent subsets such as 7, 11, 21, or 22 views.
- Plugin dashboard widgets and connector interactions are separate systems; current audits do not prove every view control has an equivalent semantic operation.

## Required architecture

- Generate the inventory from registered declarations/AST/manifests rather than hand-maintained arrays.
- Stable unique view ID, route, package/plugin owner, slots, operation IDs, backing action/API/use-case, required role/capability, chat/voice projection, interaction/widget kind, mobile/desktop states, and test/evidence links.
- Detect duplicate IDs/routes, unreachable views, missing owner, orphaned control, direct business mutation in view code, unclassified authorization, and missing interaction projection.
- Produce machine-readable CI artifact and human-readable report.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] Inventory exactly matches runtime registration in every supported target/build mode.
- [ ] Documents and every other ID/route collision is resolved deliberately.
- [ ] Adding/removing a view updates generated output automatically.
- [ ] Every mutating control maps to one typed operation and authorization decision.
- [ ] Every operation declares chat, voice, secure link-out, or explicit view-only justification.
- [ ] CI rejects collision, orphan, stale hand-list, missing gate, and missing evidence classification.
- [ ] Debug/internal views are explicitly excluded from production or governed and tested.

## Tests and evidence

- Generator determinism, runtime reconciliation, synthetic duplicate/orphan/missing-gate failures, build-target differences, and package export changes.
- Publish the complete 22-view report and reconcile it against actual navigation, runtime registry, action/API registrations, and screen captures.

## Dependencies

Foundational for the two following view issues and consumes the role/capability gate inventory.

## Current-source audit and implementation plan — 2026-09-06

Baseline: `8fea4f94c29c7712ce3caf303e3350150622d048`; read-only source and live issue/PR review. This is a plan, not implementation or runtime certification.

### What already exists; corrections to historical claims

- PR [#24141](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/pull/24141) merged on 2026-08-22. An AST-backed generator already traverses first-party package entrypoints, resolves declarations, rejects malformed static declarations, and checks normalized per-modality ID/path collisions. Extend [the existing discovery owner](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/8fea4f94c29c7712ce3caf303e3350150622d048/packages/scripts/lib/plugin-view-inventory.mjs#L945-L986); do not create another inventory generator. Root `audit:plugin-view-inventory` is already included in `verify`.
- JSON/Markdown serialization already includes declaration provenance, owner, route, modality, related action names, capability IDs, a role minimum and surface capabilities ([row contract](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/8fea4f94c29c7712ce3caf303e3350150622d048/packages/scripts/lib/plugin-view-inventory.mjs#L739-L824)). These are declaration facts, not proof of business operation reachability or authorization.
- The old “no authoritative generated artifact” and “plugin Documents still registers the duplicate ID” claims are obsolete. `plugin-documents/src/plugin.ts` contributes HTTP routes without a `views` registration. Its public component exports remain; an export is not evidence of a second shipped route. Current `App.tsx` relationships fallback is an unavailable-state component while the plugin owns the contributed view; the older claim of two independently lazy-loaded relationship implementations must not be carried forward without a current caller.
- The historical 22/39 counts are not current acceptance constants. No current count was successfully generated in this checkout.
- The exhaustive ledger described in #24280 is **not proven delivered by its closed state**: [PR #24343](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/pull/24343) closed on 2026-08-22 without merging. Its old operation/site counts and draft artifact hashes are historical; no current operation-ledger generator was found in `packages/scripts`. Review its ideas and disposition, but do not automatically resurrect its large AST heuristics or treat its old unresolved counts as today's backlog.

### Verified remaining gaps and limits

1. The existing inventory is the source-declaration universe, not an enabled target/runtime snapshot. Discovery accepts `repoRoot`/`files`, with no target, runtime mode, installed plugin set or caller context. It cannot by itself prove the issue's “every supported target/build mode” reconciliation. Catalog emission additionally filters caller role and restricted-platform dynamic loading ([actual catalog boundary](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/8fea4f94c29c7712ce3caf303e3350150622d048/packages/agent/src/api/views-routes.ts#L532-L548)). Shell aliases and app registrations require reconciliation against their own production owners.
2. `operationIds` comes from declared capability IDs; absent related actions/capabilities become empty arrays, and absent role gate becomes `null`. There is no control-to-use-case, per-operation authorization decision, input/output/error contract, idempotency, projection, evidence or responsive-state proof in that row. A passing collision audit cannot satisfy the semantic coverage acceptance criteria.
3. Runtime registration uses exact `(id, viewType)` keys and warns/keeps the existing owner on conflict; the static audit normalizes case/slashes. It does not use the runtime collision policy or verify actual route reachability. Dynamic third-party registrations are outside the first-party AST scan ([registration](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/8fea4f94c29c7712ce3caf303e3350150622d048/packages/agent/src/api/views-registry.ts#L321-L368), [normalized collision check](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/8fea4f94c29c7712ce3caf303e3350150622d048/packages/scripts/lib/plugin-view-inventory.mjs#L911-L943)). Define the desired runtime policy explicitly rather than assuming CI protects arbitrary installed plugins.
4. The existing agent integration inventory test independently scans `views:`/`componentExport:` source text and reconstructs declarations; it is not an independent observed runtime registration of the full production target ([test harness](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/8fea4f94c29c7712ce3caf303e3350150622d048/packages/agent/src/__tests__/plugin-view-inventory.test.ts#L52-L100)). Reuse production registration for runtime reconciliation, while retaining intentional negative parser fixtures.
5. Direct `registerPluginViews` calls return for missing/empty views before removing prior entries. Deeper caller review found that both production reload wrappers unload/unregister first, so this is not evidence of a normal hot-reload failure. Keep a real reload regression, and investigate a direct-helper replacement only when an affected caller is identified. The independent review below supersedes the earlier lifecycle suspicion.

### Ordered implementation

1. **Re-establish executable baseline.** In a dependency-complete checkout at the implementation head, run the existing inventory and save generated JSON/Markdown outside Git. Record source 8fea4f94c29c7712ce3caf303e3350150622d048, generator version, actual declaration count, excluded packages and their reasons. Compare two generations byte-for-byte. Do not install or copy an old generated inventory merely to obtain a green row.
2. **Add target reconciliation to the existing owner.** Derive enabled registrations from actual desktop/web/mobile/restricted-store/AOSP build and runtime inputs; boot the real registry for each supported variant and capture `/api/views` under representative authorized roles. Compare sets and routes using explicit classifications: registered-and-available, intentionally excluded, unavailable bundle, alias, internal/developer/preview. Include prebuilt shell registrations and aliases once. Fail on unexplained missing/extra owners and route conflicts; preserve legitimate per-modality distinctions.
3. **Set collision and lifecycle contracts.** Choose and document rejection versus supported replacement, normalize identically at source and runtime boundaries, and prove rejected registration does not leave a half-replaced catalog. Cover nonempty-to-empty and unload transitions. Verify Documents has one actual route owner; preserve published exports unless their compatibility/removal decision is separately approved. Only remediate a current Relationships collision if the target reconciliation demonstrates one.
4. **Define a typed operation ledger from production registrations.** Reuse existing view capability/action/agent-element and app-shell/overlay owners. Each actionable control needs a stable semantic operation ID, owner/use-case, typed request/result/error, authority requirement, confirmation/idempotency behavior, channels, and evidence classification. Keep secure OAuth/credential link-outs and genuinely local view-only affordances explicit. Do not infer semantic equivalence from matching method names or generic verbs, and do not blanket-promote every GUI capability to chat/voice.
5. **Calibrate the sprint slice on real operations.** Exercise Calendar create/edit, Notes create/edit, Todos complete/reopen and Settings save, including denied and failing backing operations. Join actual rendered enabled controls to their declared operations and their real backing action/API; record unresolved joins as failures, not fabricated view-only classifications. Use measured work per unresolved boundary to re-estimate the full ledger rollout. The existing 2-day allocation covers an inventory/contract foundation, not remediation of all controls across all targets.
6. **Wire incremental enforcement and handoff.** Emit machine-readable and human-readable unresolved entries with source/control locations, target, owner, reason and evidence. Fail CI for duplicate/unowned/unclassified entries and stale production-registration joins. Coordinate existing domain owners instead of creating coverage-only/per-file issues. Feed the same navigation rows to #30682 and semantic execution rows to #23111/#24104; define the remaining parent acceptance explicitly before claiming this broad issue complete.

<!-- guardian-project19-adversarial-2026-09-06:start -->
## Independent adversarial review #23110 — 2026-09-06

Exact source `b676280384b15df54204276e34bb8740db26936f`; reviewed previous plan against actual host registration/unload/reload and catalog/search consumers. No provider or repository mutation.

## P2 — Earlier empty-array stale-registry hypothesis is not a reproduced production reload defect

Direct registerPluginViews does return before removing old entries for an empty array ([packages/agent/src/api/views-registry.ts:321](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/b676280384b15df54204276e34bb8740db26936f/packages/agent/src/api/views-registry.ts#L321)). But both production lifecycle installations explicitly unload first: [packages/agent/src/runtime/plugin-lifecycle.ts:934](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/b676280384b15df54204276e34bb8740db26936f/packages/agent/src/runtime/plugin-lifecycle.ts#L934) and [packages/agent/src/runtime/plugin-lifecycle.ts:1243](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/b676280384b15df54204276e34bb8740db26936f/packages/agent/src/runtime/plugin-lifecycle.ts#L1243) call unloadPluginOperation before registerPluginOperation. The former unload removes views at line 926; the latter at 1232. Same-name registration short-circuits through base registration rather than invoking the registry as a replacement. This disconfirms the implied normal hot-reload route to the helper-level defect. Existing helper test at views-memory-lifecycle line 339 (actual file view-memory-lifecycle.test.ts) tests only adding an empty set, not replacement.

Amendment: retain helper idempotency cleanup only as bounded contract work; first add a real runtime.reloadPlugin(nonempty → empty) regression and expect the existing production path to remove the views. Do not call the ordinary reload lifecycle broken without a caller that bypasses unload. Inventory/alias work must not be blocked on a speculative repair.

## P2 — Define inventory isolation because the current registry is process-global

Registry is a module-level Map keyed only by modality/id ([packages/agent/src/api/views-registry.ts:59](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/b676280384b15df54204276e34bb8740db26936f/packages/agent/src/api/views-registry.ts#L59)); unregister removes every matching pluginName without runtime identity. Lifecycle synchronization operates per-runtime. Therefore the proposed multi-target/runtime reconciliation cannot safely assume registering two runtime snapshots into one process gives independent inventories. A second runtime's registration/unload can replace/remove the same plugin's rows in that shared map. This is direct data-structure/caller evidence, not an allegation of deployed cross-tenant access.

Amendment: run target fixtures in isolated processes or explicitly reset/own registry lifecycle before each snapshot; record host instance/runtime/plugin configuration in artifact identity. Separately decide whether the product supports multiple independently configured runtimes sharing one registry. If yes, design scoped ownership rather than merely labeling snapshots with runtime IDs; if no, assert the host invariant. Test two simultaneous runtimes before proposing any namespace migration.

## Search/lifecycle counterevidence examined

Background embedding writes lack a registration-generation fence (views-search-index.ts:74–98), but normal plugin lifecycle calls registerPluginViews without runtime (plugin-lifecycle.ts:907/1201), so no plugin embedding job is scheduled there. Also /views/search joins scores against current listViews, so a removed stale embedding alone does not resurrect a removed catalog entry (views-routes.ts:449–505). Do not escalate this as a current navigation leak. If new inventory/alias work enables embedding on live reload, cover late old-generation completion overwriting new descriptors and current-list join.

Prior generator/type-owner findings were scrutinized against catalog role filtering and search's current registry join; this review adds no new declaration-count claim. Required evidence remains actual registration/target reconciliation, not static parser counts.

## Implementation checkpoint: live operation discovery and evidence

Work remains in progress; this does not satisfy the full inventory acceptance criteria. The Notes operation migration is still an uncommitted implementation undergoing review.

- Notes create/edit action and view adapters now invoke the actual service-owned executors. Each executor parses its input and returns the existing persisted domain result plus a durable effect receipt; adapters preserve their established wire envelopes. Existing owner gates consume the same gate definition. Discovery is local introspection, not a remote invocation API or independent authorization mechanism.
- Runtime discovery joins those executor objects to actual plugin/service ownership, runtime identity, and service generation. It reports unregistered, partial, stopped, duplicate and mismatched catalogs as explicit gaps. Retaining empty service buckets now reports an empty runtime instead of healthy coverage. Stopped or unloaded Notes executor handles reject further writes; reload creates a new generation.
- `report:runtime-operations` boots the explicitly configured headless runtime and waits for deferred registration. Its new snapshot records revision, dirty state, runtime ownership and unresolved gaps under the canonical `reports/runtime-operation-inventory/` producer. The normal evidence ingestor handles this producer. Dirty source, failed boot phases and registration gaps return nonzero; the report always marks rendered projection execution and supported-target coverage unverified.
- A real runtime/Notes filesystem integration executes a persisted mutation, writes the snapshot, excludes older producer output using the pre-run baseline, builds and verifies the bundle, proves later producer changes do not mutate bundled evidence, and rejects tampered bundle bytes. This tests the storage and evidence boundaries, not GUI or live-model selection.

Validation at this WIP checkpoint: Notes 9 files / 116 tests passed; live runtime inventory tests 4 passed; API reconciliation tests 2 passed; agent, Notes and evidence typechecks and lint passed. The full evidence suite passed 51 files / 532 tests with 2 existing skips after repairing a separate FFmpeg compatibility defect. FFmpeg 9 removed `-vsync`; the isolated one-line `-fps_mode vfr` repair was independently verified on real two-scene video with both bundled FFmpeg 6 and system FFmpeg 9. That repair will ship separately. Full root validation, full affected core/agent suites and independent review of the new operation contracts remain required.

Provider/native stubs cannot satisfy provider/native acceptance.

Isolate processes while the existing view registry is process-global; labeling shared mutable snapshots with different runtime IDs does not provide isolation.

A contract-name string, static declaration count, or direct executor call cannot establish rendered control coverage or model selection.

Preserve existing declaration/collision ratchets until that replacement is demonstrably stronger. 18:T3ba2,# [cloud][billing][e2e] Prove recurring billing with real Stripe Test Clocks and document operations

## Summary

Add a gated, non-production real Stripe certification lane that proves recurring lifecycle, allowance accounting, dunning, plan changes, cancellation, deletion interaction, UI, and operational recovery. Mocked Stripe tests are necessary but insufficient.

## Safe Test Clock authority

- Add a server-only Test Clock binding accepted only when an explicit E2E flag is enabled, the deployment is non-production, and the Stripe key is test mode.
- Production hard-fails if either test binding/flag is present.
- Customer authority attaches the clock only at test customer creation; no client header or provider-ID escape hatch.

## Required matrix

1. New Plus checkout and paid invoice grant exactly $25 allowance.
2. Duplicate/reordered webhook delivery does not double-grant.
3. Advance one month: expire remainder once and grant the next period once.
4. Failed renewal: past-due/grace behavior and notices.
5. Advance beyond retries/grace: free entitlement and over-limit creation block without deletion.
6. Payment-method recovery restores entitlement without duplicate allowance.
7. Cancel at period end and undo.
8. Paid Plus-to-Pro proration and time-prorated allowance delta.
9. Pro-to-Plus change effective next period.
10. Customer Portal return and application reconciliation.
11. Refund, dispute, clawback, and negative-debt handling.
12. Account deletion cancellation prevents renewal and stale-event restoration.

## Evidence requirements

- Poll asynchronous Test Clock advancement; do not use arbitrary sleeps.
- Inspect Stripe Customer/Subscription/Invoice objects, webhook queue/DLQ, database subscription/entitlement/allowance/funding/invoice rows, API responses, and UI state.
- Run real Checkout and Customer Portal UI through Playwright/local stack.
- Archive screenshots, MP4, structured backend logs, frontend console/network trace, and redacted DB exports outside the repository.
- Update billing/rate-limit/API/deploy/runbook documentation with allowance, overage, cancellation, dunning, refunds, reconciliation, and operational alerts.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] Every matrix row passes against real Stripe test mode and current application code.
- [ ] Evidence validator rejects stale, missing, mocked, skipped, or secret-bearing bundles.
- [ ] Package tests/typecheck/lint, Cloud E2E, app audit, root verification, and evidence review gates pass.
- [ ] Test-only bindings cannot be enabled in production.

## Dependencies

Final certification issue; depends on all seven preceding subscription issues and the account-deletion billing fence.

## Critical implementation audit — 2026-09-06

Source: `8fea4f94c29c7712ce3caf303e3350150622d048`. This is certification design and current-source research, not executed Stripe or deployment evidence.

### Existing harness is not real recurring certification

[Stripe client](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/8fea4f94c29c7712ce3caf303e3350150622d048/packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/stripe.ts#L37) pins API `2024-11-20.acacia`. Its CLOUD_E2E override is an explicit loopback endpoint with synthetic test key; it must remain labeled mocked/local evidence, not a real Stripe sandbox. Current source search found no Test Clock attachment path or generic trial lifecycle. Existing [durable operation tests](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/8fea4f94c29c7712ce3caf303e3350150622d048/packages/cloud/shared/src/db/repositories/subscription-billing-operations.pglite.test.ts#L1) and Stripe queue tests are prerequisites but cannot prove provider lifecycle or delivered UI/notice state.

The Sprint 3 matrix revises the original $30/$100/no-trial scenario to $29/$99, card-required seven-day first trial; keep old catalog/grant revisions in separate compatibility cases. Do not silently carry the original $25/$90 grant amounts into the new revision until its policy is ratified. #30638, #23090–#23094, #22930, #30565 and deletion integration must supply runnable accepted contracts first. #25316 remains OPEN at research time.

### Detailed certification implementation

1. **Build explicit readiness manifest.** Record exact app/API/worker/runtime source and deployed build, database migration revision, Stripe API/SDK version, environment, approved catalog/price revision, policy revision, generic app/account identity, infrastructure payer identity and named dependencies. Run read-only staging catalog preflight before mutation. Fail a run whose environment or source cannot be identified; unavailable catalog, unresolved policy or missing trial authority is blocked, not a skipped passing row.
2. **Add isolated real-provider harness.** Use server-only sandbox/test-mode clock configuration with explicit non-production gating; reject it in production and reject client-supplied clock/customer IDs. Attach clock at creation through canonical customer authority and isolate test customers from ordinary accounts. Keep synthetic loopback tests separate. Use the installed pinned Stripe API's Customer path; current documentation also shows newer Accounts APIs, which are not an instruction to migrate this integration. [Stripe's Test Clock guide](https://docs.stripe.com/billing/testing/test-clocks/api-advanced-usage) describes clock-linked customers and asynchronous advancement; poll clock/provider completion and app reconciliation receipts rather than sleeping an assumed duration.
3. **Run hosted Checkout and actual app flow for both plans.** Capture card collection, trial dates/selected price disclosure, successful trial, decline/authentication-required, abandonment, double submit and response-loss recovery. Verify authoritative subscription/trial identity and pending/active UI after the signed webhook is reconciled. Confirm zero trial invoice never creates a paid allowance grant. Include navigation away/back and process restart. Account authorization must exercise actual subscriber account rules and separate existing org OWNER/ADMIN billing controls, not require buyers to administer the infrastructure account.
4. **Advance lifecycle and assert accounting.** At seven-day expiry observe first attempted charge, invoice/payment status, trial-to-paid entitlement and exactly-once paid grant. Advance monthly renewal, failed renewal/recovery, cancel/undo/resubscribe, upgrade failure and successful paid proration, scheduled downgrade and policy-approved refund/dispute/debt cases. Replay signed duplicate/reordered deliveries, omit a delivery then run reconciler, and inspect queue/DLQ/stuck commands. Original trial identity must survive every retry and plan change. Keep historical catalog compatibility as a separate matrix column.
5. **Certify Dedicated effect continuity.** Test delayed/failed provisioning and recovery, same-instance/data continuity at trial conversion, and policy-approved expiry/dunning/cancel/deletion access. Join provider invoice, application subscription, entitlement, allowance/funding allocation, instance and user-visible status by stable run/command identity. Recovered checkout/webhooks must not duplicate a charge, grant or instance. After deletion, late events cannot restore entitlement. Do not infer instance health from a payment success screen.
6. **Capture independently assessable evidence.** For each matrix row retain real provider object identities in protected evidence, signed-event/reconciliation provenance, redacted authority/ledger exports, API headers/responses and actual desktop/mobile UI, MP4 plus backend/frontend console/network logs. Record notices separately from subscription state. [Stripe's free-trial page warns that sandbox trial reminders are not sent](https://docs.stripe.com/payments/checkout/free-trials?payment-ui=stripe-hosted), while other customer-email guidance can describe restricted test-delivery exceptions. Resolve the exact account/environment configuration and prove controlled delivery directly; never treat Test Clock success as an email-delivery receipt or claim delivery impossible from one page alone. Prove application-owned notices through their real test transport and identify Stripe-owned reminder acceptance separately.
7. **Gate and document operations.** Evidence verifier must reject missing/stale/mock/secret-bearing or silently skipped records. Run owning-package tests/typecheck/lint, Cloud E2E, app audit, root verify and canonical bundle review. Document exact replay/reconciliation, alert ownership, stale command recovery, source-accounting readback and policy-approved rollback/cancel runbooks. Cleanup only the run's isolated sandbox artifacts, retaining required receipts. Publish per-row passed/failed/blocked status without claiming all rows from a partial run.

Capacity boundary: 2.5 days is a ready-system certification slice, not implementation time for missing generic app billing, lifecycle, provisioning or policy. Full original matrix remains acceptance scope; if dependencies are late, deliver the reproducible lane and explicit blocked rows, not a fictitious completed certification. No production charge, refund, deployment or resource mutation is included in this planning update.

<!-- guardian-project19-adversarial-2026-09-06:start -->
Independent adversarial review at `b676280384b15df54204276e34bb8740db26936f`. Live issue read; this supplements the earlier plan. Source-proven means the checked-in logic establishes the observation, not that a deployed provider incident was reproduced. No provider/infra mutations or dependency installation.

### P1 — Do not equate queue acknowledgement with lifecycle application

The actual Stripe processor returns ack for unknown event types. Its current switch lacks invoice and subscription lifecycle handlers; a real signed recurring event can therefore traverse the queue successfully without creating subscription authority. A qualification script checking webhook HTTP200/queue ack or an empty retry queue would certify a no-op. [Dispatch](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/b676280384b15df54204276e34bb8740db26936f/packages/cloud/api/src/queue/stripe-event.ts#L133).

Amendment: every scenario must join the provider event ID to its durable recurring receipt, applied subscription revision, resulting entitlement source, and expected exact grant/ledger transaction. Require the handler's explicit applied or intentionally ignored disposition; unknown unsupported events must never count as lifecycle coverage. Include a control fixture that omits the recurring handler and prove the certification fails despite HTTP/queue success. Also assert duplicate delivery produces one mutation, while a different legitimate invoice for the same subscription produces the next appropriate grant.

### P2 — Existing catalog preflight is not a reproducible certification receipt

runSubscriptionCatalogPreflight reports catalogVersion, plan keys, count of objects and wrongFixtureRejected. It does not identify checked objects, bound Stripe account, environment deployment, validation time, code/config digest or API version. approvedProviderObjects is calculated as plans.length*2, not observed retrieval count; getVerifiedSubscriptionPlans may reuse its five-minute process cache. A later reviewer cannot distinguish a fresh provider read from a cached result or match the receipt to a concrete dashboard configuration. [Receipt](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/b676280384b15df54204276e34bb8740db26936f/packages/cloud/scripts/admin/preflight-subscription-catalog.ts#L83), [cache](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/b676280384b15df54204276e34bb8740db26936f/packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/services/subscription-catalog.ts#L430).

Amendment: add a dedicated read-only certification mode with explicit cache bypass or recorded verification timestamp/cache provenance. Persist a secret-free manifest binding environment/account, object IDs or approved redacted references, API version, catalog/config digest and actual validation results. Preserve strict wrong-fixture rejection. A warm-cache test must show the receipt is honest about lookup freshness. The CLI's injectable provider remains valuable unit coverage; mark simulated-provider receipts as such so they cannot be promoted into real Stripe evidence.

### P1 — Add adversarial state ordering and deployment-version cases to Test Clock acceptance

A clock advancing a happy-path invoice is insufficient for the source seams discovered in #23090/#23093/#23094. Include restart across catalog rollout while a purchase outcome is unknown, reverse completion of two provider retrievals, replay of a prior event after a newer lifecycle transition, lease expiry, and deadline passage without a webhook. Verify persisted command price identity, current-source entitlement finalization and terminal ledger conservation. ## Summary

## Required states

- Available Plus/Pro plans and current selection.
- Current allowance versus purchased non-expiring credits.
- Renewal date and period usage.
- Upgrade pending payment, downgrade scheduled, cancellation scheduled/undo.
- Past due, action required, grace deadline, unpaid/canceled, provider unavailable, and webhook-lag reconciliation.
- Member read-only with explicit owner/admin requirement.
- Checkout/portal return and retry/recover.

Use only server DTOs. Do not embed Stripe IDs, calculate allowance/limits client-side, or fabricate healthy values on fetch failure. Preserve top-up, crypto/PAYG where supported, auto-top-up, invoices, active resources, and account limits.

## Critical source audit and implementation plan — 2026-09-06

Audited develop `8fea4f94c29c7712ce3caf303e3350150622d048`, live PR state, UI/app guides and current Billing callers. #30546 is MERGED (`536e6ff24009646fb99d30f51d1d2b84b9a8d6d7`); #26752 is still OPEN (`f5fb96bdf1bfdc33d2b23e151873c328a7d915b2`). The inspected checkout is clean, so the prior issue's dirty-file warning is historical and must be rechecked at implementation time. No checkout, browser flow, payment or visual audit was run during research.

### What already works in source and what remains

- **Canonical Billing reuse is implemented.** [BillingSectionBody](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/8fea4f94c29c7712ce3caf303e3350150622d048/packages/ui/src/cloud/billing/BillingSection.tsx#L142) owns freshly confirmed account identity and mounts `BillingTab`; Settings adapters and the standalone billing route consume it. Preserve this shared body rather than introducing another subscription page/state machine.
- **Existing body has useful financial UI and recovery.** [billing-tab.tsx](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/8fea4f94c29c7712ce3caf303e3350150622d048/packages/ui/src/cloud/billing/components/billing-tab.tsx#L1) renders server snapshot balances/compute, card and crypto top-ups, auto-funding and invoices. It differentiates paused/loading/error/stale observations and uses exact money formatting. Its card checkout coordinator binds durable intent to organization/user/amount, guards provider URL navigation and retains idempotency across ambiguous failures ([lines 590–626](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/8fea4f94c29c7712ce3caf303e3350150622d048/packages/ui/src/cloud/billing/components/billing-tab.tsx#L590)). These are top-up flows, not implemented recurring plan selection/management.
- **Required recurring UI is absent from this body.** No subscription catalog/status fetch or trial/renewal/upgrade/downgrade/portal lifecycle renderer was found in BillingTab. [Public plans route](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/8fea4f94c29c7712ce3caf303e3350150622d048/packages/cloud/api/v1/subscriptions/plans/route.ts#L28) already validates server-only Stripe bindings and returns unavailable on failure; use it. The public lifecycle DTO currently lacks trial and several required management/action fields; #23095/#23091 must provide them before production controls can truthfully render.
- **Requested offer differs from current source.** [Current catalog](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/8fea4f94c29c7712ce3caf303e3350150622d048/packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/services/subscription-catalog.ts#L94) defines Plus $30 and Pro $100, while this sprint asks for $29/$99 and a card-required seven-day trial. Catalog/lifecycle owners must version and validate that offer against actual provider bindings. Do not hardcode new prices, trial length or inferred charge dates into UI to conceal the mismatch. Resource ceilings, funding, trial and dunning decisions remain with product owners and #30638/#23090/#23094; #22930 specifically owns reversal/refund/chargeback hold and restoration policy.
- **One current honesty defect is visible in source.** [BillingSection.tsx:293–299](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/8fea4f94c29c7712ce3caf303e3350150622d048/packages/ui/src/cloud/billing/BillingSection.tsx#L293) displays “Payment canceled. No charges were made.” from the checkout-canceled return predicate, while the checkout coordinator correctly notes that redirects/cancellation are not authoritative outcomes. A return URL alone cannot prove no charge. Reproduce that UI condition with an ambiguous server outcome and replace the financial claim with neutral return/reconciliation state unless server verification establishes the outcome.

### Ordered implementation plan

1. **Freeze the server-to-UI contract with #23095/#23091 and catalog owner.** Require plan/version/price/trial disclosure, actual next charge/currency/timezone, allowance and purchased balances, usage/reset, current/effective/pending lifecycle states, role-derived allowed commands and safe action URLs. Include explicit stale/unavailable/provisioning/payment-action-needed states. Reconcile #26752's exact live branch before editing shared payment components; do not duplicate its provider-neutral state layer.
2. **Extend the canonical body with recurring plans and status.** Present approved comparison data from the catalog DTO and keep purchased credits/crypto/PAYG/top-up/invoices/compute/limits intact. Place card-required trial conversion and recurring charge disclosure adjacent to the relevant CTA, with actual end timestamp and cancellation entry. Use server decisions for eligibility, caps, grace and amounts. For managed organization billing, member read-only state explains who can manage; server checks remain authoritative on click. Keep #30638 app subscriber/account management separate: a buyer must not need merchant or infrastructure organization administrator privileges to manage their own subscription.
3. **Implement management as durable server intents.** Checkout, upgrade, pending payment, scheduled downgrade, cancel/undo, explicit resubscribe and portal each carry operation identity and lifecycle revision. Extend the existing coordinator patterns for cross-tab/double-click/reload recovery without treating top-up intent shape as interchangeable with recurring subscription changes. Role/account switch invalidates old UI actions; a late response cannot redirect or apply state for a different account. Preserve ambiguous operations for reconciliation instead of minting new payment attempts.
4. **Make returns and failures truthful.** Back/refresh/expired/abandoned checkout, 3DS cancellation, provider unavailable, webhook lag and network interruption retain selected plan and operation identity. A URL flag is only navigation context. Poll/reconcile the exact server operation to terminal or explicit unresolved state, expose manual retry/resume where safe, and never assert active access or no charge from a redirect. Cancellation is distinct from refund and chargeback; the UI must not promise an unapproved refund.
5. **Accessible interaction and safety.** Reuse UI primitives, safe navigation URL guard, error boundaries and focus return. Render status with text/icon as well as color; announce async outcomes and preserve keyboard focus across dialogs/redirect recovery. Use orange accents and darker-orange hover, 44px touch targets, mobile layouts, contrast and reduced motion. Keep server/provider identifiers and internal diagnostics out of product copy.

Assert server intent identity, one provider attempt, rendered actionable outcome and focus—not copied labels/CSS tokens. Include the cancellation-return honesty regression above.

Record MP4, before/after full-page captures, console/network/backend logs in a verified evidence bundle. No checkout or visual success is claimed by this audit.

Planning: three days assumes usable catalog/lifecycle/status/management APIs and #26752 reconciliation. ## Independent adversarial review — 2026-09-06

Re-reviewed `b676280384b15df54204276e34bb8740db26936f`, live issue and prior plan, BillingSection/BillingTab/success-page callers, complete checkout intent reserve/bind/clear flow and relevant tests, and the real create-checkout route/order replay. This review made no payment, browser navigation or repository edit.

### P2 — The existing coordinator's “TTL” deliberately does not expire a same-amount intent

[reserve](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/b676280384b15df54204276e34bb8740db26936f/packages/ui/src/cloud/billing/lib/card-checkout-intent.ts#L996) returns an existing same-user/same-amount intent before checking time. After the 25-hour boundary, changing amount raises a reconciliation error instead of creating a new request. [Existing tests](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/b676280384b15df54204276e34bb8740db26936f/packages/ui/src/cloud/billing/lib/card-checkout-intent.test.ts#L296) explicitly require this behavior. Therefore the new recurring-flow plan must not treat the exported TTL as a safe operation-expiry/rotation rule or fix stale state by deleting browser storage.

The actual [server order create](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/b676280384b15df54204276e34bb8740db26936f/packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/services/stripe-checkout-orders.ts#L197) returns the existing durable idempotency winner after matching digest/initiating user. [The route](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/b676280384b15df54204276e34bb8740db26936f/packages/cloud/api/stripe/create-checkout-session/route.ts#L360) retrieves and returns the existing session for delivered/settled orders without translating session lifecycle into a typed resume/completed/expired operation disposition. At BillingTab lines 539–554 a missing session URL becomes a generic error while the intent remains. This traces a concrete missing recovery contract for cases where the provider can no longer offer the original session URL; it is not a claim that every return or elapsed 25 hours fails.

Amend Step 3/4: add a server-owned reconciliation result that distinguishes paid/fulfilled, resumable, authoritatively terminal-unpaid, still uncertain, and unavailable. Permit replacement only through an explicit reconciled terminal transition bound to the original operation/principal; a timeout, browser cancel flag or elapsed local TTL is insufficient. Give this recovery a visible action rather than leaving the user with a non-resumable URL error indefinitely. Do not reuse amount-only top-up identity for recurring price/catalog version, trial acceptance, change type and expected subscription revision.

Acceptance: a real persisted order with a provider session that is no longer resumable produces truthful next steps; uncertainty preserves the original key; an authoritative completed operation never creates another charge; terminal-unpaid replacement gets an intentional new identity; stale/different-amount conflict has a usable reconciliation path. Include two tabs and account switch. This is a lifecycle test across route/order/UI, not an assertion of the TTL constant.

### Counterevidence — Important race guards already exist and should be retained

[BillingTab](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/b676280384b15df54204276e34bb8740db26936f/packages/ui/src/cloud/billing/components/billing-tab.tsx#L481) checks captured user/organization and local attempt generation before/after async work, passes principal preconditions to the server, binds the exact session and refuses a superseded bind before navigation (lines 573–605). [The endpoint](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/b676280384b15df54204276e34bb8740db26936f/packages/cloud/api/stripe/create-checkout-session/route.ts#L98) verifies the supplied rendered principal before catalog/order/customer/Stripe work. Do not describe account-switch redirect prevention as wholly absent; extend these proven seams to recurring operation types and current server authority.

[BillingSuccessPage](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/b676280384b15df54204276e34bb8740db26936f/packages/ui/src/cloud/billing/BillingSuccessPage.tsx#L150) uses request generations and requires verification `success: true`; exact-session cleanup occurs afterward and cleanup failure stays distinct from a verified payment. Its component is keyed by user/session/source (lines 340–380). This contradicts a blanket claim that both success and cancellation screens trust redirect flags. The earlier identified **cancellation banner** still does: BillingSection lines 31–33 and 293–299 use presence of `canceled` to assert no charges. Correct that specific claim and prove ambiguous return state; preserve the server-verified success path.

### Dependency and evidence limits

#23095 must supply coherent permitted commands, charge/date/lifecycle observations, and receipt-aware notices; #30638 supplies buyer account authority separately from merchant-org managers. #23090/#23094 own approved offer/trial/funding semantics. UI changes cannot resolve unknown provider state by showing an invented free trial, price, balance or deadline. No full UI suite or browser flow was executed in this source review. Required app visual cycles and controlled provider evidence remain outstanding implementation gates, not audit completion evidence.

1a:T46fe,# [cloud][billing] Expose secure subscription status, settings, audit, and lifecycle notifications

## Summary

Expose a complete, safe organization subscription summary and lifecycle communication contract while hardening existing money/resource mutations that currently accept overly broad organization membership or API-key authority.

## Status contract

Return current plan/state, allowance used/remaining/expiry, purchased balance, total spendable, renewal date, pending cancel/downgrade, grace/dunning action, portal availability, and server-derived limits. Never expose Stripe IDs, payment details, provider configuration, or secrets.

## Authorization

- Organization members may read the shared billing summary where policy permits.
- Checkout, portal, change, cancel, billing-settings mutation, and billable-resource cancellation require session-only OWNER/ADMIN authority.
- If billing automation through API keys is retained, add a narrow explicit capability/scope; do not accept general organization keys.

## Notifications and audit

- Idempotent in-app/email notifications for payment failed, action required, retry/grace deadlines, renewal success, upgrade/downgrade/cancel scheduled/effective, and recovery.
- Stripe emails supplement but never replace application state.
- Audit actor, command, organization, provider outcome, decision, and correlation without secrets.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] Cross-organization/IDOR reads and mutations fail.
- [ ] MEMBER/GUEST/general API key cannot mutate subscription or billing resources.
- [ ] OWNER/ADMIN authorization is rechecked at final mutation boundary.
- [ ] Stale role/session/CSRF/replay attempts fail without provider calls.
- [ ] Status distinguishes loading/unavailable/dunning from healthy zero values.
- [ ] Notifications dedupe, escalate, and resolve correctly.
- [ ] DTO/log/telemetry secret scans pass.

## Tests and evidence

- Authorization matrix, IDOR, stale membership, CSRF, replay, provider-unavailable, DTO snapshots, notification dedupe/escalation/recovery, and audit-redaction tests.
- Real two-user organization evidence showing member read-only and owner/admin management.

## Dependencies

Depends on the lifecycle and entitlement APIs; consumed by the Billing UI.

<!-- guardian-sprint3-2026-09 -->
## Sprint 3 proposed slice

Add exact trial end, next charge amount/date/timezone, pending conversion/cancel/change, payment action and access end to server DTOs. Proposed cadence for owner confirmation: signup receipt, 72h pre-trial-end, 3-day pre-renewal, success/failure and effective cancellation. Use durable deduplicated notice events; suppress obsolete reminders and correct date/amount after allowed plan changes. Stripe built-in seven-day trial reminder is not sufficient by itself for a useful late reminder in a seven-day trial, and Stripe does not send trial reminder emails in sandbox. Acceptance: duplicate/reordered events, delayed delivery, cancellation, changed timezone and card failure produce correct single notifications plus actionable in-app state; independently inspect controlled delivery evidence, not merely Test Clock event receipt. Preserve existing owner/admin/read-only/audit requirements. Official sources: https://docs.stripe.com/billing/revenue-recovery/customer-emails and https://docs.stripe.com/payments/checkout/free-trials?payment-ui=stripe-hosted . This draft sends no messages.
