## Critical source audit and implementation plan — 2026-09-06

Audited develop `8fea4f94c29c7712ce3caf303e3350150622d048`, live issue/comments and related PR state. #24235 is now MERGED at head `b44d34903e5463db515674a39e7aab7336fd14b5`; its cancellation-authority slice is already represented in current source. This audit performed no billing mutation, Stripe account inspection, email send or live authorization test. Proposed signup/72-hour trial/three-day renewal cadence remains unratified product policy.

### Current implementation and missing contract

- **Reuse the existing secure cancellation path.** [billable-resource cancellation](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/8fea4f94c29c7712ce3caf303e3350150622d048/packages/cloud/api/v1/billing/resources/%5Bid%5D/cancel/route.ts#L10) consumes `requireCurrentBillingManagerSession` and durable cancellation receipts. #24235's comment is historical implementation evidence, not proof of this issue's complete status/settings/notices scope or fresh two-user acceptance.
- **A primary organization-scoped billing snapshot already exists.** [limits route](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/8fea4f94c29c7712ce3caf303e3350150622d048/packages/cloud/api/v1/billing/limits/route.ts#L34) derives organization from authenticated context, reads the canonical primary snapshot, and projects fresh cancellation authority. Existing snapshot v2 supports unavailable/unknown-policy observations. Extend/reuse its ownership and freshness semantics rather than exposing raw billing database rows or recomputing balances in the UI.
- **Subscription lifecycle projection is only a partial type seam.** [SubscriptionDto](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/8fea4f94c29c7712ce3caf303e3350150622d048/packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/types/cloud-api.ts#L464) describes active/grace/past_due/unpaid/canceled, periods, pending plan and allowance; it lacks trial, next charge/currency/timezone, portal/action availability, exact access end and notification status. Searches found its SDK mirror but no complete live status consumer. Coordinate schema/lifecycle changes with #23091 rather than rendering a fictional trial state locally.
- **Existing billing-settings admission still needs the scoped authorization review named by this issue.** The settings mutation has not adopted the same current billing-manager authority seam as resource cancellation. Complete that hardening with the auth owner and retain detailed security reproduction in the private security process; do not assume the merged cancellation PR covered all financial settings.
- **No durable subscription notice implementation was identified.** [Stripe queue dispatch](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/8fea4f94c29c7712ce3caf303e3350150622d048/packages/cloud/api/src/queue/stripe-event.ts#L133) handles checkout, payment-intent and refund/dispute events but does not dispatch subscription/trial/invoice lifecycle events in this switch. Existing email service has welcome, low-credit, auto-top-up and purchase emails, not the required subscription sequence. Existing queue/event dedupe and billing revision persistence are reusable foundations; event receipt alone is not notification delivery.
- **Correct the blanket sandbox-email claim.** Current [Stripe customer-email documentation](https://docs.stripe.com/billing/revenue-recovery/customer-emails) describes the seven-day trial reminder and says sandbox emails can be sent to verified-domain or active-team-member addresses. The [Checkout free-trial page](https://docs.stripe.com/payments/checkout/free-trials?payment-ui=stripe-hosted) still states trial reminders are not sent in sandbox. These primary pages disagree on the exception; qualify the evidence and test the controlled account/recipient. Do not infer delivery from Test Clock events or claim blanket sandbox impossibility. No account-specific behavior was tested here.

### Ordered implementation plan

1. **Agree the server status and notice policy with lifecycle owners.** Define trial end, next charge amount/currency/date and source timezone, access end, scheduled conversion/cancellation/change, dunning action/grace, allowance versus purchased balance, portal availability and permitted commands. Make pending, stale, unavailable and unratified policy explicit. Do not derive the next charge from catalog price when proration, tax, credit or a pending change can alter it. Resolve timezone fallback and notice recipients, channels, grace rules and cadence before enabling sends.
2. **Finish authoritative reads and management authorization.** Add the status projection/API using tenant-bound primary authority and immutable lifecycle revision, with fresh role/session checks at each management effect boundary. For organization billing, reuse current OWNER/ADMIN cancellation authority; general member/API-key management is not implicitly approved. Keep this distinct from #30638 app subscriber/account authority: a buyer manages their own subscription through that account-bound authority and must not need merchant or infrastructure organization administrator privileges. If automation remains supported, specify a narrow capability separately. Scope cache keys to organization/role/revision and invalidate on role/lifecycle changes. Return only safe public fields and capability reasons; keep provider IDs and payment details server-side.
3. **Build durable notices from canonical lifecycle transitions.** Persist notice intent with organization, subscription revision, notice kind, effective period, recipient/channel and dedupe identity; use the existing scheduling/queue architecture, not timers in the UI or an independent scheduler. Send only after reading current canonical state and checking supersession. Reordered events, changed charge amount/date, cancellation, recovery and timezone changes must replace or suppress stale reminders. Persist send attempt, acceptance/receipt and outcome; unknown provider acceptance must not blindly duplicate a message.
4. **Unify actionable in-app state and email.** Derive both from the same revision and allowed next actions. A send failure cannot roll back subscription truth or make Billing show healthy state. Failed-payment notices escalate only under approved policy and resolve on canonical recovery. Generate authenticated management links through the existing portal/checkout boundary; do not include Stripe customer IDs, secrets or reusable bearer URLs in ordinary DTOs/logs.
5. **Audit decisions and integrate #23096.** Record actor/organization, command and idempotency identity, prior/resulting revision, authority decision, provider correlation and outcome without sensitive payloads. Expose per-channel notice state and safe retry/recovery, while the UI renders server amounts/deadlines/capabilities. Reconcile generic billing owner #30638, policy #22930 and staging binding #30565 before claiming deploy readiness.

## Independent adversarial review — 2026-09-06

Re-reviewed `b676280384b15df54204276e34bb8740db26936f`, the live issue/first plan, organization snapshot read and route, email service and actual welcome/low-credit callers, lifecycle queue dispatch, SDK DTO mirror and billing UI dependency plans. No message send, payment, database mutation or deployed authorization test was performed.

### P2 — Existing email transport cannot supply the receipt contract the plan requires

[EmailService.send](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/b676280384b15df54204276e34bb8740db26936f/packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/services/email.ts#L119) returns `false` when uninitialized. In both SMTP and SendGrid branches it awaits transport delivery submission but discards the returned result and returns `true` (lines 127–168). Template methods propagate that boolean. Thus simply enqueueing the existing `send…Email` call and marking `true` as “delivered” loses the provider correlation needed for ambiguous-send reconciliation and recipient delivery evidence. This is an interface gap for the new notice contract, not proof that an existing reminder was sent twice or never delivered.

Amend Step 3 with a typed dispatch result at the actual mail boundary: configured/unavailable, rejected, accepted with safe provider/message correlation, and uncertain acceptance. Persist those separately from independent delivered/bounced/recipient-observed evidence. Retain compatibility for old boolean consumers deliberately rather than silently changing their truthiness semantics. Test the new outbox against the actual adapter result mapping and persistence; exercise missing configuration, accepted-then-write-failed, transport timeout with unknown acceptance, and subsequent reconciliation. A boolean-returning stub cannot prove the new receipt contract. Reuse existing scheduling ownership and approved policy; do not promise exactly-once external delivery where the selected provider/adapter cannot prove it.

### P2 — A coherent existing snapshot is not yet a coherent subscription snapshot

[The canonical read](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/b676280384b15df54204276e34bb8740db26936f/packages/cloud/shared/src/db/repositories/account-billing-snapshot.ts#L146) already takes its observation timestamp from the database transaction and reads organization balances/revisions and resource/auto-funding sources within that transaction. [Its route](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/b676280384b15df54204276e34bb8740db26936f/packages/cloud/api/v1/billing/limits/route.ts#L34) separately supplies current authority and runtime tier observations. This is good counterevidence to inventing a new browser-computed billing snapshot. However, the read's organization projection contains balance revisions, not the future generic app subscriber lifecycle/notice revision. The new status cannot truthfully share an observation/revision label merely because several independently fetched pieces were spread into one DTO.

Amend Step 1/2 to define consistency and freshness per authority: read subscription/account state, pending operation, next-charge source and notice intents under an explicit coherent revision or label their separate observations/staleness. Keep next-charge unknown while provider invoice/operation evidence is unresolved; do not infer it from a current plan label. Role-dependent permitted commands still require fresh effect-boundary checks. #30638 owns app-subscriber account authority; do not attach merchant organization's financial details or manager role to a buyer's status. The existing organization's OWNER/ADMIN cancellation contract remains its own authority.

### Decisions, counterevidence and acceptance not supplied by this audit

- Source inspection confirms no full subscription notice pipeline in the checked Stripe queue switch; ordinary welcome/low-credit sends are real existing callers, not reusable proof of subscription cadence. Neither their current existence nor a test-clock event establishes recipient delivery.
- Retain the earlier controlled-account qualification for conflicting Stripe sandbox-email documentation; this review did not repeat external delivery testing or change that finding.
- #22930 owns reversals/refunds/chargebacks/holds/restoration, not all notice cadence, trial eligibility/start or dunning product decisions. #23091/#23094/#30638 must furnish the underlying lifecycle before #23096 can render it. Missing policy must be unavailable/pending, not a fabricated deadline.
- Required acceptance adds: server status and notice intent reference the same approved charge/lifecycle revision; a superseded reminder never sends; a successful provider acceptance does not become “delivered” without corresponding evidence; an unavailable mail adapter is visible without corrupting subscription truth. Existing authorization hardening findings remain private; no additional public exploit claim is made.

1b:T38b6,# [cloud][billing] Derive every enforced rate and resource quota from the subscription entitlement projection

## Summary

Make one server entitlement service authoritative for inference rate limits, resource ceilings, storage, API snapshots, and every create/start/resume boundary. Eliminate subscriber limit flapping caused by remaining purchased-credit balance.

## Implementation

- Active/grace subscription projection overrides legacy cumulative-credit classification; audited manual overrides remain highest authority.
- Cache payloads carry entitlement and catalog versions; lifecycle writes invalidate/hydrate them.
- Refactor inference rate limits and canonical cloud-character, agent-sandbox, container, app, and storage quota helpers.
- Make create/start/resume enforcement and `/billing/limits` consume the same service result.
- Include all four RPM fields and explicit unavailable/error state.
- Downgraded over-limit resources remain visible; block new create/start/resume instead of deleting or silently stopping.
- Cache unavailable/warming remains fail-closed for inference.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] Free, Plus, Pro, grace, past-due, unpaid/canceled, and manual override states return exact declared limits.
- [ ] A subscriber's limits do not change as allowance or purchased balance is spent.
- [ ] Every enforcement path equals the public limit snapshot.
- [ ] Stale cache versions cannot override newer entitlement state.
- [ ] Downgrade over-limit behavior is consistent across every resource kind.
- [ ] Reads do not mutate or reconstruct entitlement ad hoc.
- [ ] Admin overrides are bounded, audited, versioned, and cache-invalidating.

## Tests and evidence

- State/precedence truth tables and route/helper parity tests.
- Concurrent webhook plus create/start/resume, stale cache, cache outage, downgrade, grace expiry, and manual override tests.
- Real API evidence showing exact Plus/Pro headers and resource enforcement before and after renewal/downgrade.

## Dependencies

Depends on catalog, persistence, split funding, and lifecycle reconciliation.

## Critical implementation audit — 2026-09-06

Source: `8fea4f94c29c7712ce3caf303e3350150622d048`. Source research only; no resource provision, provider mutation or runtime enforcement acceptance is claimed.

### Existing code and concrete integration gaps

[Entitlement derivation](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/8fea4f94c29c7712ce3caf303e3350150622d048/packages/cloud/shared/src/db/repositories/subscription-entitlements.ts#L80) reads immutable subscription/catalog revisions and distinguishes effective active/grace from ineffective past_due/unpaid. Paid resource ceilings are explicitly null; canceled/expired derives free values. This is a persisted projection foundation, not complete enforcement. It has no generic app trial state.

[Rate-limit service](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/8fea4f94c29c7712ce3caf303e3350150622d048/packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/services/org-rate-limits.ts#L1) still resolves legacy credit-ledger tiers and manual overrides; its cache payload contains tier name and four RPMs, without entitlement/catalog revision fields. [Sandbox quota](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/8fea4f94c29c7712ce3caf303e3350150622d048/packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/constants/agent-sandbox-quota.ts#L1) still varies with remaining purchased-credit balance. [Container quota](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/8fea4f94c29c7712ce3caf303e3350150622d048/packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/services/container-quota.ts#L17) delegates to transactional repository checks, while [billing snapshot](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/8fea4f94c29c7712ce3caf303e3350150622d048/packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/services/account-limits-snapshot.ts#L1) assembles quota sources. Therefore changing only the public snapshot or entitlement repository would leave enforcement inconsistent.

### Bounded Dedicated slice plus broader enforcement plan

1. **Set the product/payer contract.** Consume #30638 trial/paid subscriber entitlement and #23090's new revision. Record included compute/storage/usage and infrastructure payer independently from app subscriber. Resolve trial allowance/start and expiry/dunning access/retention with product owners and #30638 before provisioning/cancel behavior is enabled; #22930 specifically owns reversal/refund/chargeback hold/restoration policy. Do not replace null ceilings with the issue's historical recommended numbers, infinity or zero. Explicit unavailable entitlement must be visible and fail closed at new admission.
2. **Build one server resolution path.** Merge audited manual override, effective subscription/trial projection and legacy non-subscriber fallback with explicit precedence and time validity. Return four RPM fields, all resource ceilings, funding/access state, catalog/source/projection revisions and availability. Reads consume committed projections; lifecycle owners rebuild them. Check effective_until/grace boundaries using authoritative time rather than trusting a stale status label.
3. **Update cache and admission atomically.** Version cache records; reject older revisions and mismatched catalog identity. Invalidate/hydrate on lifecycle and override writes, retain warming/unavailable inference behavior and prevent a slow old hydration from replacing a newer entitlement. Use the same resolved contract for headers and /billing/limits. A subscriber spending allowance or purchased balance must not change subscription limits.
4. **Integrate every resource boundary.** Trace and update cloud-character, sandbox, container, app and storage create/start/resume paths, including recovery/admin/API variants. Preserve existing transactional quota reservations/locks; checking a DTO before a separate write is insufficient under concurrent requests. Recheck entitlement revision at admission or use a version-fenced transaction. Downgrade behavior follows the ratified contract: visibility is not permission to create/start/resume, and no unapproved deletion/stop is introduced.
5. **Wire Dedicated provisioning/recovery.** Have the existing provisioning lifecycle consume the generic entitlement and retain a stable resource identity through trial-to-paid. Surface pending/failed provision distinctly from healthy active service. Retry/reconcile the same provisioning command; never start a second instance because payment or webhook delivery was retried. Reuse #25146 Dedicated-to-Shared recovery and #23098 deletion/resource ownership. Do not create another scheduler or biller; paid allowance versus cash-only runtime costs must follow the source-accounting policy.
6. **Validate at real boundaries.** Add state/precedence tests through resolver consumers, concurrent lifecycle plus resource-create tests, stale hydration/override/cache-outage tests, and spending-without-tier-flap regressions. Verify every snapshot/header matches actual admission for the same revision. In #30565 staging prove real trial provision, delayed/failed recovery, trial-to-paid instance/data continuity and policy-approved expiry/dunning/cancel behavior with instance state, authority/ledger and UI readbacks. Inspect logs and complete receipts; run shared/API/SDK checks/root verify and app audit for UI changes.

Estimate: two days covers the Dedicated integration after upstream generic authority, catalog, funding and policy decisions. The issue's all-resource/all-route enforcement sweep is broader and needs its own acceptance inventory. A passing rate-header test does not certify storage/start/resume behavior or real instance continuity. Independent adversarial review at `b676280384b15df54204276e34bb8740db26936f`. Live issue read; this supplements the earlier plan. Source-proven means the checked-in logic establishes the observation, not that a deployed provider incident was reproduced. No provider/infra mutations or dependency installation.

### P1 — Projection publication must validate current lifecycle authority

Before wiring the new quota resolver, strengthen rebuild's current-source contract. It locks the subscription but selects only its ID, then derives from the caller-selected immutable revision. Its CAS protects the projection writer revision, not the current subscription revision. Current-source monotonicity and account-wide active subscription identity therefore require an explicit guard. No production rebuild caller exists in the checked source, so this is a source-proven primitive/integration defect to fix before lifecycle activation, not a claim of deployed webhook exploitation. The detailed counterexample is kept in a private review artifact. [Source lookup](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/b676280384b15df54204276e34bb8740db26936f/packages/cloud/shared/src/db/repositories/subscription-entitlements.ts#L155), [projection CAS](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/b676280384b15df54204276e34bb8740db26936f/packages/cloud/shared/src/db/repositories/subscription-entitlements.ts#L200).

Amendment: atomically derive from the current authoritative subscription and reject mismatched requested source identity/revision. Guard organization-wide authority across replacement subscriptions, not only monotonically increasing integers within one subscription. Retain provenance for terminal/free transitions so diagnostics can explain which authoritative transition produced them. Test delayed work, replay, replacement subscriptions and CAS retries against a live database; prove no prior observation supersedes a later authoritative transition.

### P1/P2 — Grace expiry is not represented in the derived entitlement interval

The derivation sets entitlement_effective for grace but always uses current_period_end as effective_until. The existing test fixture has grace_expires_at August27 and current_period_end September1, and explicitly expects September1. Thus a future consumer checking only projection effective_until would treat August28 as still effective despite the stored grace deadline. Conversely, a grace period allowed beyond period end would stop prematurely. Current ordinary rates remain legacy tier-derived, so this is not proof they already enforce the wrong date. [Derivation](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/b676280384b15df54204276e34bb8740db26936f/packages/cloud/shared/src/db/repositories/subscription-entitlements.ts#L90), [existing test](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/b676280384b15df54204276e34bb8740db26936f/packages/cloud/shared/src/db/repositories/subscription-entitlements-derivation.test.ts#L20).

Amendment: specify the approved status-dependent entitlement deadline and include it in the canonical projection; trial_end, grace_expires_at and paid period bounds cannot share an unqualified current_period_end rule. Null/invalid required dates must produce an explicit unavailable authority state. Probe exact equality, time just before/after deadline, grace shorter/longer than billing period and absent lifecycle worker. Test the consumer's actual admission decision with a controlled clock, not only equality of derived fields.

### P2 — Expiry must constrain cache lifetime, even without a revision change

Inference admission hydration obtains balance, legacy tier and entitlement concurrently, stores only a subscriptionFunded boolean from plan_key, and copies tier rates. It carries neither entitlement effective interval nor source/projection revision. Adding revision-key invalidation alone is insufficient: a deadline can pass without any row changing, and an older in-flight warmer can republish after invalidation. [Hydration](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/b676280384b15df54204276e34bb8740db26936f/packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/services/inference-admission-snapshot.ts#L44).

Amendment: carry the domain decision's source revision, validity deadline and account authority revision. Require cache writes to reject older generations and cap validity at the earliest authoritative deadline. At use, fail closed or rewarm if the deadline passed; do not rely on delayed webhooks or a fixed TTL. Coordinate balanced admission and funding decisions rather than reinterpret a non-free plan name as effective paid authorization. Acceptance must pause an old warmer, update authority, warm the new state, resume the old warmer, and cross the deadline without a webhook.

Research: read schema/FKs, authority replay, complete rebuild and actual inference/AI billing readers. Schema foreign keys prove revision existence/tenant identity, not recency. Existing Bun derivation test was attempted but dependency loading failed on drizzle-orm; concurrency and real admission proof remain unexecuted.

1c:T3d65,# [cloud][billing] Implement Stripe subscription checkout, Customer Portal, changes, and webhook reconciliation

## Summary

Implement the complete Stripe recurring lifecycle with session-only owner/admin mutations, durable commands, authoritative-object reconciliation, safe upgrade/downgrade/cancel semantics, and healing for missed or reordered webhooks.

## API and authorization

- Thin Hono routes for checkout, portal, change, cancel, and status.
- Mutations require a user session plus explicit organization OWNER/ADMIN role; no API key or ordinary member.
- Server chooses organization, customer, price, return URL, portal configuration, and idempotency key.
- Enforce CSRF, strict rate limits, safe app-origin returns, and one active checkout/subscription per organization.

## Provider lifecycle

- Checkout uses `mode: subscription` and canonical Stripe customer authority.
- Checkout binds a durable command; it never grants allowance.
- Upgrade activates only after successful paid proration and grants a time-prorated allowance delta.
- Downgrade and cancellation default to period end and are reversible before the boundary.
- Customer Portal is locked to approved plans/cancel behavior.
- Account deletion invokes an idempotent immediate subscription cancellation outbox and prevents later portal/webhook reactivation.

## Webhooks and reconciliation

Handle subscription checkout, `customer.subscription.*`, `invoice.paid`, payment failure/action-required/void/finalization failure, and relevant refund/dispute events. On every lifecycle event, retrieve and validate authoritative Customer, Subscription, and Invoice objects. Validate customer-to-organization, approved price, amount, currency, period, and livemode. Metadata is correlation only. Fence stale events and reconcile from current provider state.

Only validated `invoice.paid` opens a new allowance period. Add a scheduled stale-subscription reconciler, unknown-price/duplicate-subscription alerts, stuck-command report, and existing queue/DLQ integration.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] Checkout/portal/change/cancel are idempotent and recover from provider timeout or response loss.
- [ ] Unknown or cross-organization provider objects fail closed and alert.
- [ ] Duplicate/reordered webhook delivery cannot double-grant allowance or regress state.
- [ ] Upgrade entitlement appears only after proration payment succeeds.
- [ ] Downgrade/cancel timing matches the published contract.
- [ ] Missed/DLQ events converge through scheduled reconciliation.
- [ ] Account deletion prevents renewal, upgrade, portal reactivation, and stale-event entitlement restoration.

## Tests and evidence

- Route IDOR, member/API-key denial, stale membership, CSRF, idempotency, return-URL, and concurrent checkout tests.
- Provider timeout, ambiguous success, duplicate/reordered events, stale object versions, price drift, wrong currency/mode/customer, upgrade failure, cancel undo, and account-deletion races.
- Real Stripe test-mode object and webhook evidence; Test Clock certification is completed in the dedicated E2E issue.

## Dependencies

Depends on catalog, persistence, and split funding. Integrates with account-deletion lifecycle work.

## Critical implementation audit — 2026-09-06

Source: `8fea4f94c29c7712ce3caf303e3350150622d048`. Source/live GitHub-state research only; no provider operations or live acceptance claimed.

### What exists versus the missing lifecycle

[Durable subscription operations](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/8fea4f94c29c7712ce3caf303e3350150622d048/packages/cloud/shared/src/db/repositories/subscription-billing-operations.ts#L1) already provide command, event-receipt, incident and deletion-fence CAS/replay primitives. [Stripe documents](https://docs.stripe.com/payments/checkout/free-trials?payment-ui=stripe-hosted) trial configuration on subscription_data and collection of the post-trial payment method. Confirm collected card/authoritative subscription status before activating the trial under the agreed start policy. Abandoned, declined or authentication-required checkout must not appear active. A success URL or metadata is correlation, not payment/entitlement proof. Resubscribe, retries and plan changes must not reset the original eligibility record.
4. **Reconcile from provider objects.** Extend signed queue processing to the subscription/invoice events and retrieve current Customer/Subscription/Invoice state. Check account linkage, approved historical/new revision, environment, currency, item/period and actual payment state. Record event receipts and lifecycle revisions atomically with the appropriate grant/projected state. Trial grants and zero-value trial invoices must never create a paid allowance period. Fence stale events and deletion before entitlement publication. Preserve the existing purchased-credit ledger and split-funding accounting.

Queue stale/stuck commands for reconciliation using existing scheduler/queue/DLQ ownership; do not add an independent scheduler. Wire #23098/#25316 deletion fence only after exact-head integration and prove stale provider events cannot reactivate a deleted account.

Run API/shared/SDK tests/typecheck/lint, generated-route checks and root verify.

Scope boundary: four days is the Dedicated integration slice after #30638/#23090/#22930/#30565 and required persistence/funding contracts are available. The broad org lifecycle, deletion convergence and complete real-provider certification cannot be assumed covered by that estimate. Missing policy is a launch blocker, not permission to encode guessed grace/refund/resource defaults.

Independent adversarial review at `b676280384b15df54204276e34bb8740db26936f`. Live issue read; this supplements the earlier plan. Source-proven means the checked-in logic establishes the observation, not that a deployed provider incident was reproduced. No provider/infra mutations or dependency installation.

### P1 — Give reconciliation one transaction owner and a current-source finalization contract

The earlier plan says to atomically apply receipt, lifecycle, allowance and entitlement. Current public repository methods cannot simply be composed to obtain this: authority.advance and entitlement.rebuild each begin their own writeTransaction, while markEventApplied uses its own dbWrite statement. A crash between commits leaves a durable lifecycle without matching projection/receipt, and a recovery must understand that partial state. A function called "reconcile" wrapping those calls does not create atomicity.

Amendment: define a transaction-scoped internal domain operation under one documented lock order for receipt ownership, organization fence, current subscription, invoice grant and entitlement. Keep provider retrieval outside database locks. On source/CAS conflict, retrieve current provider state again; never update the expected revision while reusing an older observation. If a single transaction is intentionally impossible, specify a durable replayable state machine and make each intermediate state admission-safe. Test crash at every commit boundary, worker lease expiry during provider retrieval, and two valid provider observations completing in reverse order. [Authority transaction](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/b676280384b15df54204276e34bb8740db26936f/packages/cloud/shared/src/db/repositories/subscription-authority.ts#L360), [projection transaction](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/b676280384b15df54204276e34bb8740db26936f/packages/cloud/shared/src/db/repositories/subscription-entitlements.ts#L138), [receipt finalization](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/b676280384b15df54204276e34bb8740db26936f/packages/cloud/shared/src/db/repositories/subscription-billing-operations.ts#L662).

Replay also returns the current subscription together with the recorded historical event revision. These are intentionally different identities. Callers must not treat the returned revision as current entitlement authority. Require monotonic/current subscription-source validation in the projection owner before exposing this new lifecycle path; detailed local source counterexample is retained in the private concurrency review. No existing production rebuild caller was found. [Replay result](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/b676280384b15df54204276e34bb8740db26936f/packages/cloud/shared/src/db/repositories/subscription-authority.ts#L400).

### P1 — Recurring delivery must bypass the legacy string-based permanent-error classifier

processStripeEvent acknowledges unknown event types and acknowledges exceptions whose message contains "not found", "Invalid", or "already processed". New recurring handlers delegated to this function can permanently lose a transient reconciliation failure solely because of text; a retryable provider lookup can contain "not found" while local authority is not yet ready. This is a source-proven classifier behavior, not proof that today's absent recurring handler has lost a payment.

Amendment: use typed recurring outcomes tied to durable receipt state. Acknowledge only a durably applied/explicitly ignored/quarantined event. Retry transient provider/DB/lease dependencies independent of message wording. Test an invoice arriving before its subscription row, lookup errors containing each legacy substring, unknown supported recurring event dispatch, and crash after durable application before queue acknowledgement. Keep legacy unrelated payments behavior under its own compatibility tests. [Queue](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/b676280384b15df54204276e34bb8740db26936f/packages/cloud/api/src/queue/stripe-event.ts#L133).

### P2 — Trial activation is an authority-state migration, not only a schema enum

requireActivationAllowed currently checks only active/grace. Adding trialing to enums without including it in activation/fence/customer checks leaves different authority semantics for a state intended to grant benefits. Audit every live-subscription uniqueness predicate, findCurrent query, activation condition and allowance eligibility together. Test trial creation racing account deletion/fencing and trial-to-paid reconciliation with unchanged account ownership. Trial buyers need their own subscriber authority, not merchant infrastructure OWNER/ADMIN status.

Validation limit: existing Bun entitlement test fails before execution because drizzle-orm is unavailable; no provider calls or PostgreSQL concurrent sessions were run.

1d:T63f,## Parent / scope

Follow-up to #22942. Make every advertised container charge either enforced with a receipt or removed from the product contract.

## Problem

Container constants advertise deploy, image, monthly reference, storage, bandwidth, and extra-instance charges, but the audited production callers only establish daily CPU/memory/count billing. These are ghost prices until code and ledgers prove otherwise.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] Inventory every container rate constant and its authoritative caller/ledger/receipt.
- [ ] Obtain product approval for exact prices and units; do not infer missing rates.
- [ ] Remove unused rates and UI/docs promises, or implement idempotent metering and settlement with exact numeric tests.
- [ ] Expose only actually active charges through the canonical snapshot and active-billing API.
- [ ] Prove replay, partial failure, and concurrent billing behavior for each retained charge.

## Non-scope / overlap fence

Do not modify #22553's selected/deleting/failed billable semantics or #22906's container-delete reconciliation.

1e:T104c,Fixes #22944. Completes the centralization #22772 started (its review named this exact gap: the fourth confinement copy in plugin-coding-tools "cannot import the new helper").

## Change

**Lift, not copy**: `packages/agent/src/api/realpath-confinement.ts` moves to `@elizaos/shared/platform/path-confinement` verbatim (null-returning fail-closed walk, errno-narrowed, J3-tagged); the three agent call sites import the shared subpath directly, no re-export shim. Deep-import only — deliberately NOT added to shared's root barrel, which browser/UI code consumes and which must not pull `node:fs`.

**plugin-coding-tools consumes it**, replacing the last fail-open walk (bare catch swallowing every errno, climbing past dangling/EACCES ancestors, lexical fallback return) and the pre-#22408 `..`-prefix `isWithin` bug (`..safe.js` false-denied inside an allowed root).

**sandbox-service policy is decided per direction, not inherited.** Entries keep BOTH spellings `{lexical, canonical|null}` and are never dropped on resolution failure — a blocklist that shrinks on error fails open, and dropping the last allow-root would silently disable the allowlist (`validatePath` skips it when the list is empty). The two matchers are deliberately asymmetric:

- **blocklist**: any-spelling × any-spelling (over-blocking is the safe direction) — pinned by an aliasing test and a post-start symlink-retarget drift test;
- **allowlist**: the CANONICAL candidate only. A symmetric matcher was tried first and the existing workspace-escape test caught it live: lexical admission re-admits a symlink whose real target escaped the root. The `?? lexical` root fallback covers an EACCES-at-init root that resolves later (pinned by a chmod test, skipped as uid 0);
- an unresolvable candidate refuses outright (`unresolvable_path`; all 8 `validatePath` callers verified to degrade the new reason to their generic branch — none switch exhaustively).

Also: deletes the exports the move orphans (`isWithinAnyRoot`, `relativeFromRoot`, `normalizeAbsolute` — zero production callers), and fixes the stale "desktop-only" comment on `core-plugins.ts` (the plugin gate excludes android/ios and store builds; this code runs in cloud dedicated agents).

## Testing

- plugin-coding-tools full suite **618/618** (38 files); packages/shared full suite 2078 passed with **3 failures identical by name on pristine develop** (`steward-session-client` — pre-existing); packages/agent batched suite 471 files with **4 failing files identical by name on pristine develop** (inbox-thread-mute, server-cors-preflight, mobile-dns, audio-redaction — pre-existing), targeted lanes for the three touched files 70/70.

Two earlier mutations that failed to bite exposed missing tests (drift, EACCES), which were added — the matrix is pinned where it is load-bearing.

## Proof

- One shared SyntheticWorld coordinator owns atomic global reset; provider-local reset preserves sibling/world evidence.
- Global reset advances every provider generation, restores identical hashes, clears per-attempt ledgers, and rejects stale sibling writes.
- Administrative credentials are cryptographically random per boot, excluded from canonical state/logs and production-client configuration; the former seed-derived credential is rejected.
- Real CloudApiClient and production OpenAI SDK clients exercise protocol-shaped loopback mocks across auth, success, empty, fault, streaming, tool, usage, 429, cancellation, concurrency, idempotency, and teardown paths.
- Fixture/fault authoring rejects ambiguous IDs/routes, invalid status/response/action shapes before mutation.
- Same-provider accounts are isolated by credential, tenant, and connection identity.
- Catalog ownership comes from the canonical runtime-surface AST inventory plus maintained in-process/Mockoon roots and binds every row to executable suite IDs.
- Mock receipts are `mock-only`; they never certify a provider.

## Summary

Consolidates the browser bridge into one security-reviewed workspace with Chrome MV3, Firefox WebExtension, and signed Safari Web Extension outputs. It adds:

- execution-time authorization against current pairing, session, host grant, policy, pause/control, incognito, and site state;
- loopback-only background discovery with status-probed candidates and origin-bound pairing;
- atomic queued-to-running companion claims, action-bound progress receipts, durable resume, and idempotent recovery;
- explicit user-gesture website permission grants and a real DNR blocked interstitial;
- Chrome for Testing and installed Firefox automation lanes plus signed Safari packaging;
- browser-session contracts shared with personal-assistant and computer-use routing.

The repair addresses every requested change on #21861: it removes the unsupported branded-Chrome load claim, restores the complete shared contract, hardens TOFU discovery, normalizes loopback origins, binds the extension API to the paired origin, removes dead claim state and the wallet shim, fixes error-policy handling, and signs Safari artifacts against an exact certificate/team.

## Risk

Medium-high. This is a privileged browser execution bridge. Pairing, action identity, origin trust, host permissions, profile ownership, replay resistance, and installed-browser behavior are security boundaries.

# Risks

Medium. This is CI enforcement and reporting code: an extraction false positive can require classification, while a false negative can omit a runtime surface. The extractor is production-registration-derived and protected by real-repository, adversarial, factory/spread, computed-key, root-layout, worker-name, package-census, and ratchet tests.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Adds the synthetic-world foundation requested by #22897:

- derives 2,165 runtime surfaces from production registrations across maintained plugins and core hosts, including factories, spreads, promoted subactions, computed model keys, platform/native exports, connectors, queues, workers, routes, and host surfaces;
- records all 107 maintained plugin/host packages, including the 10 packages that intentionally expose no runtime registration;
- classifies every surface as `covered`, `exempt`, `platform-deferred`, `provider-qualified-only`, or `unsupported-product`, always with a written reason;
- requires covered rows to bind an executable artifact and exact boundary signal;
- fails CI for new unclassified surfaces and for stale/expanded baseline debt;
- emits machine-readable JSON plus Markdown/HTML summaries grouped by owner, dependency, lane, and synthetic-world workstream;
- keeps the legacy #8801 keyless-plugin gate and extends the existing #8802 coverage report instead of replacing either contract;
- protects the baseline with CODEOWNERS and runs the production ratchet in the normal script-test lane.

## What kind of change is this?

Feature: non-breaking CI inventory, report, and compatibility enforcement.

# Documentation changes needed?

The script README is updated with the inventory schema, status semantics, commands, package census, compatibility behavior, and generated report locations.

The aggregate lane failed only in unrelated pre-existing timing-sensitive suites under extreme concurrent host load; examples include Cloud git-history tests, `run-all-tests-*` timeout controls, `run-with-deadline.test.mjs`, and evidence-review/HITL fixtures. None of those files are changed by this PR.

## What this lands

Resurrects the server-side slice of the Cloud-only iOS PKCE authentication lifecycle (closed draft #16641, `pull/16641/head` @ `a29b9de`) rebased and conflict-resolved onto current `develop`, exactly as recommended in the 2026-08-15 issue triage: the Worker routes are the first landable slice because they are testable in-repo without device hardware.

- **Mobile app-auth routes** — `GET /api/v1/app-auth/mobile/config`, `POST .../token`, `POST .../ack`, `GET/DELETE .../credentials[/:id]`, plus the `flow: "mobile_pkce"` branch on `POST /api/v1/app-auth/connect`. All fail closed behind `ELIZA_MOBILE_APP_AUTH_ENABLED === "true"` **and** a registered/approved `ELIZA_MOBILE_APP_AUTH_APP_ID`, so nothing is reachable until the deploy-time registration is provisioned (verifier script included: `packages/scripts/cloud/admin/verify-mobile-app-auth-registration.ts`).
- **S256 PKCE grant lifecycle** — one-time authorization codes bound to code_challenge/state/redirect; migrations renumbered from the stale 0177–0179 to **0269–0271** with the journal extended (develop had advanced to 0268).
- **Inactive-until-exact-ACK credentials** — `eliza_mobile_` secrets are minted inactive and excluded from every positive auth cache; only the exact ACK activates them; expiry, tombstone-based self/account revocation with durable receipts, and recovery listings are covered by the ported service + repository suites.
- **Ingress protection** — new `MOBILE_API_KEY_INGRESS_LIMITER` Cloudflare rate-limit binding (fresh namespace ids 1615304/1615314/1615324 — develop had already consumed the ids the draft used), credential-scoped limiter keys that never embed the secret, a global IP backstop on the dedicated-agent proxy fast path, and hard rejection of mobile secrets on the query/WebSocket transport.
- **Cleanup cron** — `/api/cron/cleanup-mobile-app-auth` on the existing `*/15` schedule drains expired grants and stale inactive credentials.

27:Tbb9,## Summary

Adds a first-party **Settings → Devices & Runtimes** experience for controlling account-owned Eliza runtimes from iPhone and desktop clients.

- pairs trusted controllers using QR deep links or six-digit one-use codes
- exposes local Mac, Eliza Cloud, linked desktop, and advanced SSH/VPS runtime targets
- relays signed, end-to-end encrypted commands through Eliza Cloud
- keeps SSH keys and VPS bearer credentials in native secure storage
- uses the owning Mac as the SSH gateway for VPS runtimes
- supports the existing Headscale path while retaining an encrypted Cloud-mailbox fallback
- adds host revocation, replay resistance, route allowlisting, credential migration, and runtime switching

## Security model

- pairing challenges expire after five minutes and are single-use
- controller and host identities are device-bound
- Cloud stores verifiers and opaque encrypted envelopes, not pairing codes or command plaintext
- SSH runs without a shell, binds forwarded ports to loopback, remembers first-seen host keys, and rejects changed keys
- untrusted authorization headers and non-agent routes are rejected
- scanned iOS pairing codes wait until Cloud authentication completes instead of being consumed prematurely

See `packages/app-core/docs/devices-runtimes-security.md` for the complete boundary.

- [x] GET/POST forwarding, route allowlist, and authorization stripping covered

Fixes #19899

## What

Adds two first-party provider adapter plugins, modeled on the `plugin-google-workspace` connector pattern, projecting Notion and Dropbox into the notes/documents/files capabilities. Both speak the provider REST APIs directly over fetch — no MCP runtime, per the parent epic (#19877) architecture.

### `@elizaos/plugin-notion`
- `NotionClient` (API 2022-06-28): workspace search with cursor pagination, page reads, paginated block-children reads flattened to plain text (with unsupported-block-type reporting), page creation, paragraph appends. Every read result carries the canonical `notion.so` deep link for citations.
- Typed error surface: `NOTION_AUTH_EXPIRED` / `NOTION_FORBIDDEN` / `NOTION_NOT_FOUND` / `NOTION_RATE_LIMITED` (+`retryAfterSeconds`) / `NOTION_INVALID_REQUEST` / `NOTION_MALFORMED_RESPONSE` / `NOTION_UPSTREAM_FAILURE` — callers branch on codes, never HTTP details.
- OAuth: workspace-bound authorization-code flow (`owner=user`, Basic-auth token exchange — Notion has no PKCE, refresh tokens, or incremental scopes; access is scoped by what the user shares with the connection inside Notion's consent UI). Workspace identity (id/name/icon/owner) is recorded on the account for deterministic account selection.
- Credential resolution: BYO `NOTION_TOKEN` (local/self-hosted mode) → metadata credential refs → connector-account storage → vault refs.

### `@elizaos/plugin-dropbox`
- `DropboxClient` (API v2): `list_folder`(+`/continue`), `search_v2`(+`search/continue_v2`), `get_metadata`, `download`/`upload` via the content host with ASCII-escaped `Dropbox-API-Arg`, `get_temporary_link`. Deterministic `dropbox.com` deep links (folder browse / file preview) on every entry for citations.
- File limitation UX: `downloadText` refuses binary (`DROPBOX_FILE_NOT_TEXT`) and oversized (`DROPBOX_FILE_TOO_LARGE`, 1 MB cap, checked before download) files instead of returning garbage — callers offer `getTemporaryLink` instead. Uploads default to `mode: "add"` so accidental overwrites fail.
- OAuth: PKCE + `token_access_type=offline`. Default scope set = read scopes (`account_info.read`, `files.metadata.read`, `files.content.read`) plus the explicit write-escalation pair (`files.metadata.write`, `files.content.write`); caller-supplied scopes are normalized, unrecognized/empty selections fail closed; granted scopes are recorded on the account.
- Credential resolution adds refresh handling for Dropbox's short-lived tokens: refresh grant with a skew window, in-memory caching per account, refresh rejection surfaced as `DROPBOX_AUTH_EXPIRED`.

### Shared
- Durable token persistence reuses the provider-agnostic `persistConnectorCredentialRefs` helper (subpath import from `@elizaos/plugin-google-workspace/connector-credential-refs`) — tokens land in the vault/credential store as refs, never in account metadata, logs, or error contexts (asserted by tests).
- Both plugins register a `Service` (`runtime.getService("notion"|"dropbox")`) and a `ConnectorAccountProvider` at init; runtimes without a ConnectorAccountManager still get the service in BYO local mode.
- `registry-entry.json` for both, aggregated into `packages/registry/src/first-party/generated.json` via `generate:first-party`.
- Per-plugin `CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md` (byte-identical, parity gate green) and `README.md`.
- Mock base-URL overrides (`ELIZA_MOCK_NOTION_BASE`, `ELIZA_MOCK_DROPBOX_BASE`, `ELIZA_MOCK_DROPBOX_CONTENT_BASE`) for protocol-faithful mock servers.

# Risks

Low to medium. This adds a new, provider-neutral plugin and does not register a managed provider, UI surface, scheduler, media store, or transport. The main risks are adapter response normalization and saved-place ownership/idempotency; both have adversarial contract coverage.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

- Adds `@elizaos/plugin-maps` with local guide, README, package manifest, typed exports, and plugin registration.
- Defines validated provider-neutral `PlaceRef`, `RoutePlan`, `SavedPlace`, coordinates, paging, adapter, and service contracts.
- Persists saved places through the existing runtime memory architecture with agent/entity ownership, stable IDs, serialized concurrent writes, replay receipts, and idempotency-conflict detection.
- Adds the `MAPS` umbrella action and promoted place, route, save, share, and navigation actions. Missing inputs return the canonical typed form interaction.
- Adds a generic injected HTTP adapter with bounded requests, auth-state distinction, retry metadata, strict response validation, opaque connection IDs, and redacted failures. A managed Google adapter and maps UI remain outside issue 2.1.
- Exercises the production adapter against the shared provider-contract harness and a real loopback upstream rather than mocking the adapter under test.

## What kind of change is this?

Feature (non-breaking new domain plugin).

# Documentation changes needed?

The new plugin is documented in `plugins/plugin-maps/README.md`; its contribution and architecture rules are mirrored byte-for-byte in local `CLAUDE.md` and `AGENTS.md`.

# What does this PR do?

- forwards the active action-turn signal into the public skill installer and preserves typed failures in `ActionResult`;
- owns request/response/socket disconnect cancellation across both slug routes, from before `isInstalled` through discovery, state publication, and response;
- stages discovered state and prevents late transport or state writes after disconnect;
- accepts every positive finite timeout and schedules values above Node's timer ceiling in monotonic segments;
- cancels every unused non-success body without masking the authoritative HTTP result, while reporting teardown failures;
- adds real peer-close coverage for catalog details/package and GitHub `SKILL.md`/README.

This preserves the existing boolean installer compatibility unless a caller explicitly requests typed errors.

# Risks

Medium. This changes cancellation and deadline ownership across action and HTTP install entry points. First-reason semantics, listener cleanup, discovery/state fencing, rejected-body teardown, and all remote legs have adversarial coverage.

# Documentation

The package README and paired `CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md` document the positive-finite segmented timeout and caller-cancellation contract.

# Risks

Large. This changes credential storage and one-time migration paths across macOS, iOS, renderer persistence, Telegram Personal sessions, and the Vault management UI. The implementation fails closed, uses allowlisted native slots, verifies migrated bytes before deleting legacy plaintext, and has regression coverage, but signed-device and complete interactive evidence are intentionally still pending while this PR is a draft.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

- Adds an owner-only Vault workspace for inventory, connected accounts, audit history, and protection posture.
- Adds an iOS Security.framework secure-store plugin with a fixed service, allowlisted accounts, device-only accessibility, no Keychain Sharing group, and iCloud synchronization disabled.
- Routes macOS Vault and renderer credentials through the native keyring binding instead of shelling out to the security command.
- Migrates device auth, Steward/Cloud session state, active runtime details, and agent profiles only after an exact secure-store readback; failed writes never fall back to plaintext.
- Encrypts Telegram Personal session and transient login state as AES-256-GCM envelopes using the Vault master-key policy.
- Keeps Local Vault, connector credentials, Apple native session state, and Cloud organization KMS secrets as explicit separate trust domains.
- Removes automatic scraping of Claude, Cursor, and Copilot Keychain records.
- Adds security architecture documentation and regression coverage.

## What kind of change is this?

Feature and security improvement.

# Documentation changes needed?

Documentation is required and included in docs/security/platform-secure-store.md and docs/security/vault-and-credential-trust-domains.md.

31:T1031,Fixes #19900

## What

Adds `@elizaos/plugin-spotify` — the Spotify music integration with managed OAuth and self-hosted local mode:

- **`SPOTIFY` umbrella action** with subactions: `search`, `library_list` / `library_save` / `library_remove`, `playlist_list` / `playlist_create` / `playlist_add`, `playback_state` / `play` / `pause` / `next` / `previous`, and `devices` / `transfer` (device handoff). Write subactions return receipts naming the exact mutation.
- **`SpotifyService`** owns per-account access-token resolution: managed connector accounts read `oauth.tokens` behind vault refs and refresh on expiry/401 with durable write-back; local mode refreshes from `SPOTIFY_CLIENT_ID` / `SPOTIFY_CLIENT_SECRET` / `SPOTIFY_REFRESH_TOKEN`. Account selection prefers a connected managed account, then local mode, then fails fast with `SPOTIFY_AUTH_REQUIRED`.
- **ConnectorAccountManager provider** (managed OAuth): PKCE authorization URL with fail-closed scope narrowing, code exchange, `/v1/me` identity resolution (records the free/premium `product` tier), vault-ref credential persistence (raw tokens never land in account metadata, listings, or logs), and disconnect/revoke invalidation of cached tokens.
- **Typed error vocabulary** (`ElizaError` codes): `SPOTIFY_PREMIUM_REQUIRED` (free-tier playback), `SPOTIFY_NO_ACTIVE_DEVICE` (suggests handoff), `SPOTIFY_RATE_LIMITED` (parsed Retry-After), `SPOTIFY_SCOPE_INSUFFICIENT`, `SPOTIFY_AUTH_REVOKED` (`invalid_grant` / double-401), `SPOTIFY_UPSTREAM_INVALID` (malformed payloads never become healthy defaults), `SPOTIFY_UPSTREAM_FAILED`.
- Registry entry (`kind: connector`, `subtype: streaming`, `auth: oauth`) and regenerated `packages/registry/src/first-party/generated.json`.

## Verification

- `bun run --cwd plugins/plugin-spotify test` — **49/49 passed** (5 suites): client wire-contract tests (pagination, single-retry 401 refresh, 403 premium/scope, 404 device, 429 Retry-After, malformed/unparseable payloads, designed-empty states, transport failure), token grants (PKCE + confidential, `invalid_grant` → revoked), provider OAuth start/complete against the **real core `ConnectorAccountManager`**, service credential resolution across both modes, and action routing/receipts/error mapping. Mocks are protocol-faithful Spotify responses, never a mock of the system under test.
- `bun run --cwd plugins/plugin-spotify typecheck` — clean.
- `bun run --cwd plugins/plugin-spotify lint:check` — clean.
- `bun run --cwd packages/registry generate:first-party:check` — up to date.

## Human-only remainder

Real-account evidence requires a registered Spotify application (client ID/secret at developer.spotify.com, redirect URI allow-listed, and each test user allow-listed while the app is in Development Mode) plus a live Premium account for playback-write verification. Those credentials cannot be created or exercised from CI; the wire contract is covered by the protocol-faithful suites above.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

- Defines the versioned core identity vocabulary: account-scoped claims, canonical redirects, merge/split plans, confirmations, journals, delivery resolution, and discriminated owner-binding decisions.
- Adds portable schema descriptors and real Drizzle tables with composite tenant FKs, immutable journal evidence, generation fencing, phase-specific idempotency, and active-only scoped-claim uniqueness.
- Implements a SQL identity service using one real Drizzle transaction for confirm/commit/split, preserving source principals and claims rather than destructively rewriting them.
- Removes generic confirmed `identity_link` inheritance from OWNER and manual private-access role resolution. The temporary compatibility path accepts only connector IDs carrying the verified owner-pairing marker.
- Hardens owner evaluation to require a connected, non-deleted account, matching connector, matching external subject, matching runtime instance, and a verified binding. Configured owner aliases are canonicalized.
- Keeps the service exported but cutover-gated until claim lifecycle/backfill and authenticated merge consumers land.

## What kind of change is this?

Feature foundation plus security hardening; non-breaking public additions, with intentionally stricter privilege resolution for generic relationship links.

# Documentation changes needed?

# Risks

Medium, constrained by a hard dormant boundary:

- This adds DB authority and callback-scoped secret-opening primitives, but deliberately adds no production caller or top-level export.
- The current node-history schema has no durable occurrence token. This PR therefore fails closed for any node record that has ever carried a different non-null incarnation. A separate occurrence-token migration is required before activation; this PR does not claim general ABA-safe node reuse.
- `allocated_count` ownership is not durable across shared reconciliation. The boundary must not be lifted until shared allocator ownership, settlement/release, and anti-double-count integration land in coordination with #22916.
- The vault callback is trusted, bounded, fully awaited, remote-only, and must honor its `AbortSignal`; it must not re-enter the locked DB authority.
- No migration, real KMS call, SSH, container operation, provisioning, autoscale, routing change, or deployment is introduced.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

- Reserves one caller-selected existing Docker node record and exact incarnation under a live restore claim, lease, and catalogue epoch.
- Uses the canonical multi-authority order backup → operation → lease → node → immutable node history → catalogue, then reads the primary DB clock after the final lock.
- Validates the target image against the canonical manifest-v3 digest and persists node record, incarnation, and image digest in the same transaction as the capacity CAS.
- Makes response-loss replay idempotent and rejects divergence, stale catalogue authority, invalid claims/leases, saturated or provisional nodes, target reselection, autoscale, and environment fallback.
- Keeps cross-backup restore-attempt divergence out of the blocking lease lock, closing the catalogue/lease deadlock found by the real PostgreSQL proof.
- Prevents the generic phase-advance API from writing target identity or leaving `reserved` without the complete target triplet.
- Proves the exact target before KMS unwrap, re-proves it after KMS, and executes the trusted byte callback while the second authority transaction still holds its locks. The handoff has a 1–60 second bound, a 1 second lease/claim margin, cooperative cancellation, post-callback DB-clock proof, and synchronous zeroization.
- Rejects any immutable history for a different incarnation on the selected node record. This conservative rule is intentionally availability-reducing until the occurrence-token migration lands.
- Keeps every new API definition-only until durable allocation ownership and quarantined creation are ready.

## What kind of change is this?

Feature: non-breaking, dormant repository authority for the next #20732 slice.

# Documentation changes needed?

No user-facing documentation change is needed because the API remains definition-only. The integration prohibitions are documented in JSDoc and enforced by the boundary test.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

- Hardens the centered, concise, explicit first-run permission flow and later Settings recovery without requiring Apple Speech Recognition for Cartesia-backed voice.
- Makes iOS chat keyboard/accessory behavior focus-scoped, preserves readable composer/transcript geometry, and repairs collapsed/half/full detent and rotation reconciliation.
- Keeps Home conversational by suppressing action/widget dashboard leakage from the compact chat surface and preserving the compact no-provider-text contract.
- Restores native local-notification scheduling and warm/killed tap routing to the visible Home notification center with retained, exact-once navigation intent.
- Simplifies the native App Shortcuts product surface to **Message Eliza** and **Talk to Eliza**, refreshes shortcut parameters, and preserves typed intent/deep-link authority.
- Drives Live Activity state from the canonical voice session, including truthful terminal error/end cleanup and native bridge registration.
- Adds a canonical direct/remote-Mac/APNs-off Simulator build lane for repeatable local acceptance.
- Fixes explicit Go Back navigation, repeated intentional deep links, TalkMode startup, remote-first-run fail-closed behavior, and supporting native/plugin contracts.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fixes, launch-quality improvements, and additive iOS-native product functionality.

# Documentation changes needed?

The app package guide and its byte-identical `AGENTS.md` companion document the new remote-Mac Simulator build lane.

## What

Builds the routed `/maps` web view for `@elizaos/plugin-maps`, following the established plugin-notes view architecture:

- **View declaration + app-shell registration** — `mapsPlugin.views` declares the `maps` view (`/maps`, OWNER role gate, `dist/views/bundle.js`, `MapsView` export, MAPS action affinity); `src/register.ts` + `elizaos.appRegister` register the lazy page in the signed app bundle, discovered by the manifest scan (expected-list test updated, `@elizaos/plugin-maps` added as an app workspace dep).
- **Read-only capability broker** — `MAPS_VIEW_CAPABILITIES` (`get-maps-state`, `search-places`, `get-place`, `plan-route-alternatives`, `get-saved-places`) dispatch through `serverInteract` against the real `MapsService`. Saved-place writes stay on the promoted `MAPS_SAVE` action so receipt settlement is never bypassed. Expected domain failures (invalid input, provider unavailable, not found, auth expired/revoked, rate limited) become explicit `success: false` results; systemic failures propagate to the route boundary.
- **State boundary** — `GET /api/maps/state` returns the validated snapshot DTO (providers with required attribution, provider availability, owner-scoped saved places). A missing owner entity is an explicit `unavailable` state, never a healthy-looking empty list.
- **Shared zod contract** — `src/view-contract.ts` (runtime-import-free) is parsed by both the server producer and the browser transport, so malformed payloads are rejected instead of rendered.
- **Browser bundle** — responsive map/list grid; deterministic SVG map pane (polyline decode + equirectangular viewBox projection); place details; route alternatives across all four travel modes with explicit per-mode failure rows; category filter chips; cursor pagination; saved places; attribution footer. Orange accent only. Keyboard/screen-reader coverage: labelled search form, aria-pressed filters/results, focusable SVG markers with Enter/Space activation, role=alert errors, aria-live loading.
- **Distinct states** — loading, designed-empty, transport error, offline (online/offline listeners pause search/routing), and provider-unavailable each render differently.
- **Adapter surface (additive)** — optional `MapsProviderAdapter.attribution`, `MapsService.describeProviders()`.
- **Unblocking fix** — removed duplicate `desktopHttpTransportForUrl`/`fetchAgentTransport` imports in `packages/ui/src/api/client-cloud.ts` (TS2300 on develop) that failed every plugin typecheck that path-maps `@elizaos/ui`.

## Verification

- `bun run --cwd plugins/plugin-maps test` — 7 files, **71 passed** (includes new `interact.test.ts` real-runtime broker suite over a deterministic fixture adapter: success, empty, invalid input, pagination, provider unavailable, auth expiry, rate limit, provider spoofing rethrow, partial/total route failure, unknown capability; `MapsView.test.tsx` 9 component states; `mapsData.test.ts` transport envelope/schema rejection; `geometry.test.ts` polyline + projection).

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)3a:T1429,Fixes #17025

## What

Implements the core contract + store + engine + action slice for flexible daily quota routines ("25 pushups, 3 sets a day, doesn't matter when"):

- **Shared contract** (`packages/shared/src/contracts/personal-assistant.ts`): new `count_per_day` cadence variant (`targetCount`, `unit`, `perOccurrenceWork`, `timing`) with `LifeOpsQuotaTiming` — `anytime` is structurally distinct from windows/slots and deliberately has **no** `slots`. New `LifeOpsProgressEvent`, `LifeOpsOccurrenceProgress` (required `completedCount`/`targetCount`/`remainingCount`), `RecordLifeOpsProgressRequest/Result`, and the `occurrence_progress_recorded` audit event type. Fixed-slot `times_per_day` is untouched.
- **No more fabricated slots**: `buildDistributedDailySlots` is **deleted**. The count-only paths in `buildCadenceFromLlmParams` and `normalizeCadenceDetail` now emit the quota cadence (`anytime`, or `windows` timing when the owner named windows). Explicitly named times still produce fixed slots.
- **Engine** (`engine.ts`): exactly **one** owner-local active-day occurrence per local date (`quota:<localDateKey>:day`) spanning the owner's day in the definition timezone (or the union of named windows). Next-day reset falls out of the per-day occurrence key; terminal states stay sticky; `derivedTarget` carries the quota descriptor.
- **Persistence**: new append-only `app_lifeops.life_task_progress_events` table (drizzle-pushed via the existing plugin `schema` map; plain text columns, no pg enums) with `unique(agent_id, occurrence_id, idempotency_key)`. Repository adds `appendProgressEventIfNew` (single-statement `INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING RETURNING`, mirroring `createAuditEventIfNew`), `sumProgressEvents`, `listProgressEvents`. The completed count is always re-derived from the event table — never cached on the occurrence row, so full-row occurrence upserts cannot clobber concurrent progress.
- **Service** (`definitions-service.ts` / `service.ts`): `recordOccurrenceProgress(occurrenceId, {idempotencyKey, quantity?, note?})` — idempotent append, transactional-safety via the unique constraint, completion through the existing retry-idempotent `completeOccurrence` exactly when the derived count reaches the target (streaks/performance/website-access/escalation flow unchanged), 409 from skipped/expired/muted, refusal after completion, count clamped at target in the projection.
- **Chat path** (`life.ts`): on a `count_per_day` occurrence, "I did one set" (the `complete` operation) records ONE increment keyed `msg:<message.id>` — a replayed/re-planned turn never double-counts — and replies with server-projected counts ("Logged 1/3 sets — 2 to go" / "3/3 — done for today"); target reached terminal-completes. `summarizeCadence` renders the quota honestly ("25 pushups 3 sets a day, any time") instead of fabricated `Time 1/2/3` labels. Quota definitions skip the default offset-0 reminder step (it would fire at local midnight since the occurrence starts at day start).

## Verification

- `plugins/plugin-personal-assistant` typecheck: **pass** (tsc clean)
- New `src/lifeops/engine.count-quota.test.ts` (pure, deterministic): **13/13 pass** — one occurrence per local date, anytime full-day span in `America/New_York`, windows-narrowed span, sticky terminal state + identity reuse, next-day reset, normalization bounds (target 1..100, non-empty unit, unknown timing rejected, windows validated against policy), and the LLM-param path (bare count → quota anytime; named windows → windows timing; explicit time → still fixed slot)
- New `src/lifeops/quota-progress.pglite.integration.test.ts` (real AgentRuntime + PGlite, real schema migration, no mocked repository): **4/4 pass** — increment/replay/complete-at-target with exactly 3 event rows after 4 attempts; 5 concurrent increments against target 3 never exceed or lose the target and complete exactly once; 409 from skipped state and on non-quota cadences; invalid quantity/blank idempotency key rejected
- Adjacent lanes: `engine.laddered`, `engine.unscheduled`, `definitions-service.unscheduled`, `life-reminder-datetime`, `extract-task-plan` — **175/175 pass**; `packages/shared` test suite **2076/2076 pass**; Biome lint clean on both packages

# Background

## What does this PR do?

- Adds typed, serializable per-scenario model fixtures with exact model type, input/prompt/schema/tool-set matching, cardinality, streaming/latency/error/cancellation behavior, and early supported-model validation.
- Adds attempt/world scoping, strict unmatched/ambiguous/over-consumed/unused failures, and sanitized call/consumption diagnostics.
- Adds reusable Stage-1, planner, multi-tool, clarification, reply, evaluator, scheduled-render, and adversarial fixtures.
- Serves the same registry through an OpenAI-compatible Cloud E2E adapter, including JSON/SSE tool calls, usage, provider errors, timeouts, cancellation, and bounded shutdown.
- Preserves legacy compatibility only for undeclared scenarios. Reports mark `strict-fixtures`, `model-free`, or `legacy-fallback`; declared scenarios cannot use the bridge.
- Adds a migration ratchet. Current repository count: **0 declared / 117 legacy / 117 total `pr-deterministic` scenario sources**. The legacy count may only decrease; #22901 remains open until it reaches zero.

## What kind of change is this?

Feature and test-infrastructure improvement.

3d:T129c,Fixes #14758

## Summary

Fresh current-base vertical slice of the local-only, snapshot-consistent multi-account Gmail collector, built into the existing `packages/corpus-tools` collectors pattern (per the 2026-07-22 triage refresh, which cleared `needs-human` and re-scoped this as unclaimed implementation work). Addresses every gap flagged in the branch-cleanup audit of the deleted `feat/14758-gmail-corpus-collector` branch:

- **Real Gmail History reconciliation** — a completed checkpoint is never trusted indefinitely. Each subsequent run reconciles from the checkpointed `historyId` via `users.history.list`, applying additions and deletions; an expired/invalid history id (HTTP 404/400) triggers a full rescan instead of trusting the stale marker.
- **Account-namespaced ids** — `gmail:<account>:<messageId>` / `gmail:<account>:thread:<threadId>`; shards live under `gmail/<account>/<yyyy-mm>.jsonl`, and the transport's `getProfile` mailbox must match the requested account (`GMAIL_COLLECT_ACCOUNT_MISMATCH`) for account isolation.
- **Alias-aware direction** — `From` is compared against the account plus configured `aliasEmails`; the `SENT` label is also honored.
- **Locking / durable publication** — per-account fail-closed lock (`GMAIL_COLLECT_OUTPUT_BUSY`), atomic temp-file+rename installs, byte-compare idempotent shard reuse, validated `manifest.json` written last.
- **Enforced private output** — 0600 files and 0700 directories for shards, checkpoints, staging, and state.

Core collector semantics (`packages/corpus-tools/src/collectors/gmail.ts`):

- Injected `GmailTransport` seam (profile / paginated list / full message / attachment / history); the package holds no OAuth material. A live run wraps the existing account-scoped plugin-google Gmail client with `gmail.read` scope (adapter contract documented in the package README).
- Frozen UTC window expressed in **epoch seconds** (`after:<sec> before:<sec>`) because Gmail's `after:yyyy/mm/dd` is local-timezone and would shift the frozen cutoff; chats excluded, drafts dropped by label.
- Exhaustive pagination with a durable per-page checkpoint (`data/.state/gmail-<account>.json`) plus an append-only staging file, so an interrupted run resumes at the persisted page token without refetching staged mail; torn staging tails from a crash are dropped and refetched; excluded ids (drafts/out-of-window/attachment-only) are checkpointed so incremental runs do not refetch them forever.
- Bounded 429/5xx retries with exponential backoff honoring the server `Retry-After` hint.
- MIME extraction prefers `text/plain`, falls back to stripped `text/html`; attachment bytes are fetched only to compute SHA-256 + size; attachment-only messages are counted, never given fabricated text (audit rule).
- Deterministic dedup across pages/mutation; deleted messages cannot resurrect from stale staging (staging is compacted).
- Output committed by a schema-validated manifest; `validateCorpusTarget` passes on the produced tree.

No MBOX fallback was built, per the triage instruction (only if live API evidence establishes a real blocker).

3e:T1204,Part of #19902 — read-only Linear foundation only; does not close the parent issue.

## What

First work-integration native plugin adapter for epic #19877 wave 5. Adds `@elizaos/plugin-linear`, a read-only Linear plugin exposing elizaOS actions instead of an MCP wrapper:

- **`LinearClient`** (`src/client.ts`): bounded GraphQL-over-HTTPS client pinned to a single endpoint (`https://api.linear.app/graphql` by default). Uses core's DNS-pinned SSRF guard, rejects redirects/userinfo/query endpoints, enforces one deadline and one response byte limit per request. HTTP status classification stays authoritative; Linear's GraphQL error envelopes (`extensions.code`: `AUTHENTICATION_ERROR`, `FORBIDDEN`, `RATELIMITED`) refine auth/rate-limit classification — Linear reports auth failures on `400`, which is handled explicitly. Expired vs revoked credentials remain distinct typed failures; `429` retains `retryAfterMs`.
- **`LinearService`** (`src/service.ts`): credential resolution — managed `LINEAR_OAUTH_TOKEN` (Bearer) wins over BYO `LINEAR_API_KEY` (raw Authorization, per Linear's personal-key contract); neither configured fails with typed `LINEAR_NOT_CONFIGURED`, never a fabricated-empty page. Cloud's OAuth callback already whitelists `linear` as a provider platform.
- **Actions** (`src/action.ts` + `src/plugin.ts`): `LINEAR` umbrella promoted to `LINEAR_MY_ISSUES`, `LINEAR_SEARCH`, `LINEAR_ISSUE`, `LINEAR_TEAMS` via `promoteSubactionsToActions`, all tagged `domain:work` / `capability:read`. Search filters are built from typed parameters only (title/team/state/isMe), never prose. J1 boundary translation of `LinearError` with retryable/retry-after metadata.
- All response payloads are zod-validated (`src/types.ts`); workflow-state types are a closed enum and drift is rejected, not coerced.

## Read-only by design

Per the issue ("start read-only and enable writes incrementally"), no write action ships here — no stubs either. `plugins/plugin-linear/CLAUDE.md` pins the invariant: a write requires the epic's write-policy/receipt contracts.

3f:T1559,Closes #19878.

## Summary

- add the versioned provider-neutral connected-account and capability DTOs removed by #21696, without restoring the unsafe v1 authorization model
- bind policy to an immutable selected-account snapshot, capability, operation, contextual risk, provider-owned exact-input digest, and binding time
- make both allowed and confirmation-required decisions consume-once through one trusted atomic authorization boundary
- bind the complete policy and confirmation grant digests into the in-process dispatch authority and canonical effect receipt
- keep successful, designed-empty, unavailable, and error outcomes structurally distinct without exposing provider payloads or credentials

## Security contract

- Public account IDs are opaque handles; provider credentials, request payloads, and secret material are rejected from the wire contracts.
- Contextual risk may be elevated but never downgraded below the selected account capability catalog.
- The request digest includes the selected account/provider/mode, catalog risk, requested capability/risk, operation, exact-input digest, request identity, and binding time.
- `CapabilityAuthorizationConsumer.consume` is the single trusted host boundary. Durable hosts must atomically compare-and-delete the current account/capability policy decision and its exact confirmation grant; a separate verify/delete sequence is replayable.
- Allowed decisions are one-shot too. Lack of interactive confirmation does not create reusable dispatch authority.
- The returned authority is branded, recursively immutable, and intentionally not a wire credential.
- Receipts bind the exact authorization, complete policy digest, optional confirmation digest, request/account/capability/operation/input identities, canonical `EffectReceipt`, and post-authorization chronology.

Low. The Cerebras tool path fails closed on cyclic, over-deep, over-wide,
accessor-bearing, or hostile-Proxy JSON Schema graphs before
`sanitizeJsonSchema` touches them, and the pre-pass no longer applies any
provider semantics, so declared open maps keep the `__eliza_record_entries`
reverse mapping they had on origin `develop`. Honest small tool schemas,
shared-ref DAGs, and sparse tuple keywords still behave exactly as before.
