plugin-form, catalog ReDoS, wait-for-url, secret-swap, inMemoryAdapter,
character-history, or agent-export.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

`packages/core/src/runtime/schema-compat.ts` `normalizeSchemaForCerebras` is
the live Cerebras tool-schema walk used by `plugins/plugin-openai/models/text.ts`
`normalizeNativeToolsForCall` before a planner/chat hop. On origin `develop`
it recursed with `Object.entries` and no depth/cycle cap, so a cyclic `not`
graph RangeError'd and an 8k-deep `properties` nest that `JSON.parse` already
accepted then stack-overflowed the walk.

Earlier heads bounded depth/node/cycle/accessor reads with typed
`CEREBRAS_SCHEMA_UNBOUNDED`, moved every `Array.isArray` / `getPrototypeOf` /
array-length read inside `inspectSchema`, made cycle detection path-local so
honest DAGs pass, reserved width before allocation, and moved the bounded pass
in front of `sanitizeJsonSchema` so the raw-spread sanitizer can never see an
unbounded or hostile graph.

This head fixes the compatibility defect Shaw found in that ordering. The
pre-pass is now `cloneSchemaForBoundedTransport`: the same descriptor-only
reflection, path-local cycle detection, width-reserved clone, aggregate node
budget and typed failures, but zero Cerebras semantics. Running the full
normalizer there overwrote every declared `additionalProperties` (`true` or
schema-valued) with `false`, and `sanitizeJsonSchema` only builds
`__eliza_record_entries` plus a `RecordArgTransform` when
`additionalProperties !== false` — so a tool such as
`{ customFields: { type: "object", additionalProperties: { type: "string" } } }`
reached the wire as a closed empty object with no entries field and no reverse
mapping. Sanitization now sees a plain, budgeted graph with declared semantics
intact; `normalizeSchemaForCerebras` applies provider closure afterwards, as
on origin `develop`.

This head fixes the compatibility defect Shaw found in that clone. The clone
now mirrors `walkSchemaChildren` exactly — same keyword tables
(`SCHEMA_MAP_KEYS` / `SCHEMA_ARRAY_KEYS` / `SCHEMA_SINGLE_KEYS` / `items` /
`dependencies`), same guards, same depth accounting, same aggregate node budget
and path-local cycle detection — and never descends into annotation or
extension data, which is legal arbitrary JSON that neither the normalizer nor
`sanitizeJsonSchema` walks. Those values ride across in the immutable
descriptor snapshot, so no accessor runs and nothing is capped. Depth is now
counted in schema levels, so `MAX_CEREBRAS_SCHEMA_CLONE_DEPTH` is gone and the
pre-pass reports the same `MAX_CEREBRAS_SCHEMA_WALK_DEPTH` budget as the
normalizer. The clone still covers a superset of the keywords
`sanitizeJsonSchema` recurses into, so the sanitizer's raw
spread / `Object.entries` / `.map` recursion stays bounded.

The same pass collapses the remaining doubled descriptor reads: `ownDataEntries`
takes one immutable `{key, value}` snapshot per node and `cloneOwnData`,
`mapSchemaRecord`, `dependencies`, and the clone all consume it.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (non-breaking change which fixes an issue)

## Why are we doing this? Any context or related work?

See #23198 and Shaw's reviews on #23159. Cerebras mode must fail closed with a
typed cause instead of a `RangeError`/raw `TypeError`, must not change honest
programmatic DAG schemas, and must not silently collapse declared open maps
(#11249).

# Documentation changes needed?

My changes do not require a change to the project documentation.

# Background

- Converts legacy MCP point storage only at the storage boundary and keeps fractional prices exact.
- Aligns checkout, summary, discovery, MCP routes, SDK types, UI labels, built-ins, billing matrices, and public docs.
- Adds deterministic boundary coverage for a `$1` checkout grant, fractional MCP pricing, ledger serialization, and rendered desktop/mobile UI.

# Risks

Medium, bounded by a definition-only API gate:

- No production caller or top-level export exists.
- The parent C1a schema still lacks a durable node-incarnation occurrence token. A separate migration is mandatory before activation.
- Restore capacity ownership is not durable across the shared allocator reconciler. Settlement/release and anti-double-count integration with #22916 are mandatory before activation.
- A future remote creator must be idempotent and reconcile or clean up a container when the database recorder rejects after a lease, catalogue, node, or placement drift.
- This PR contains no Docker/SSH/provider/registry call, no environment fallback, no migration, no route publication, and no deployment.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

- Opens a target activation quarantine only after the exact restore operation, claim, lease, manifest-v3 source, reserved node target, node history, and catalogue epoch are locked and re-proven.
- Derives the target activation generation from the retry-stable `restore_attempt_id`; callers cannot substitute the source generation or mint a second target identity.
- Writes only the mutable activation ledger. Canonical `sandbox_id`, `node_id`, `image_digest`, `status`, route publication, dispatch, and completion remain untouched.
- Records one already-created 64-character Docker container ID by atomically updating the quarantine ledger and the restore operation from `vault_seeded` to `container_created`, then clears the claim.
- Makes exact response-loss replay idempotent and rejects token, backup, claim, lease, catalogue, node-incarnation, placement, and container divergence without partial writes.
- Uses backup → operation → lease → sandbox → node → immutable node history → catalogue → DB-clock order.
- Removes the generic container-identity writer and blocks generic first entry into `container_created`. A retry may re-enter that phase only when the durable container ID already exists, and never rewrites it.
- Enforces a static definition-only boundary with no Docker provider, node manager, autoscaler, registry, SSH, `process.env`, or network import/call.

## What kind of change is this?

Feature: non-breaking, dormant repository authority for quarantined restore creation.

# Documentation changes needed?

No user-facing documentation change is needed because the API remains definition-only. JSDoc records the occurrence-token and allocation-settlement prerequisites.

## What this adds

**plugin-maps (`plugins/plugin-maps`)**
- New `[MAPSCARD]` chat wire contract (`src/card.ts`): place, place-list, route, share/navigate handoff, and precise-locate card payloads appended to `userFacingText`. Payloads carry display-safe fields only (no polylines, cursors beyond the pagination cursor, credentials, or provider internals).
- Per-platform navigation deep links on `MapsHandoff`: system `geo:` intent, Apple Maps universal link, and a provider-neutral OpenStreetMap web fallback (share pins vs. navigate destinations differ).
- Missing-input UI: route forms now include the travel-mode select (drive/walk/bicycle/transit); place forms include a result-limit filter; `missingFields` now reports only required fields.
- Precise location: a new `useCurrentLocation` parameter on place/route returns a locate card (`awaitingUserInput`) instead of fabricating coordinates; coordinates only ever come from the user's device via the chat surface.
- Share confirmation + receipt: `MAPS_SHARE` requires `confirm: true`. Without it, the action returns a `[CHOICE:maps-share]` confirmation and emits no share URI. Confirmed shares carry a `sharedAt` receipt rendered on the card.

**packages/ui**
- `message-maps-parser.ts`: strict validation of untrusted card payloads (coordinate ranges, string bounds, ≤10 rendered places) and a link-target policy — handoff URIs must be `geo:` or https on `maps.apple.com` / `www.openstreetmap.org`; anything else (including `javascript:`) renders as plain text.
- `widgets/maps-card.tsx` (`MapsCardWidget`, registered as the `mapscard` inline built-in): place/places result cards with directions chips, route summary card (mode, distance, duration, warnings), handoff cards with platform-preferred "Open map / Start navigation" links, copy-link, and the share receipt line, and the interactive locate card with distinct idle → locating → sent / denied / unavailable states (denied and unavailable are separate, visible error states with retry). Orange accent tokens only; chromeless per the widget contract.
- Widget matrix, registry gates, and stories updated (`WIDGET_MATRIX.md`, `inline-registry(.matrix).test.tsx`, `maps-card.stories.tsx`).

# Background

## What does this PR do?

`packages/agent/src/services/character-history.ts` `toCharacterHistoryValue`
is the live snapshot walk for `PUT /api/character` (`buildCharacterHistorySnapshot`
then `recordCharacterHistory`). On origin `develop` `e38ecc710394` it recursed
with `Object.entries`, so a cyclic `content` graph RangeError'd, an enumerable
getter was invoked, and a 20k-deep nest that `JSON.parse` already accepted
(~40 KiB) then stack-overflowed the history walk. Zod `contentSchema` is
`.passthrough()`, so extra keys are not schema-bounded.

Independent origin proof:

```text
origin develop e38ecc710394
honest messageExamples: ok 0ms
zod honest: success=true
JSON.parse extra-arr 20000 (40090 bytes): ok
zod extra-arr 20000: success=true
history extra-arr 20000: THROW 1ms RangeError Maximum call stack size exceeded
zod extra-obj 20000: success=true
history extra-obj 20000: THROW 4ms RangeError
history cyclic content: THROW 12ms RangeError
```

This head walks with descriptor-only reads, a depth/node/cycle budget, and
throws typed `CharacterHistoryError` `CHARACTER_HISTORY_UNBOUNDED` instead
of RangeError. `PUT /api/character` returns 400. Honest snapshots are
unchanged.

Occupancy-FREE hosts `packages/agent/src/services/character-history.ts` and
`packages/agent/src/api/character-routes.ts`
(`scannedAt=2026-08-20T21:45:09.712Z`). Agent leftover. Not a twin of #23127
`agent-export.ts`, #23123 `server-helpers-config.ts`, or #23115
`server-config-filter.ts`. Not leftover-tax of #23034 config-routes nest
rejection or #23054 skill-items YAML. Not a ReDoS twin of #23085 / #23076.
Not a cloud JSON 500 reprint. Not HARD_SKIP `server.ts`.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (non-breaking change which fixes an issue)

## Why are we doing this? Any context or related work?

`PUT /api/character` validates with Zod, then snapshots `messageExamples`
for persistence and the history journal. `contentSchema.passthrough()` keeps
attacker-controlled extra keys that `JSON.parse` already accepted. A legal
20k-deep nest or cyclic graph then RangeErrors the walk and 500s the route.
Fail-closed before the stack overflows.

My changes do not require a change to the project documentation.

## What

Adds `GoogleMapsAdapter` to `@elizaos/plugin-maps`: a real Google Places API (New) + Routes API integration behind the existing `MapsProviderAdapter` seam, with Cloud-managed credentials and a documented local API-key mode.

- **Two explicit credential modes, no silent fallback.** `api-key` mode calls the public Google origins directly with a server-side key in `X-Goog-Api-Key`. `managed` mode calls the Cloud maps gateway under `/google-maps/places/*` and `/google-maps/routes/*` with an opaque session bearer token plus the opaque `conn_…` connection id — no Google key ever exists in the runtime, and endpoint overrides are rejected in managed mode. Browser/server key separation is structural: the runtime key path only exists in server-side `api-key` mode and the key never appears in URLs, contexts, or diagnostics.
- **Validated normalization + attribution.** Google payloads (`places:searchText`, place details with `X-Goog-FieldMask`, `directions/v2:computeRoutes`) are schema-validated and normalized into the plugin's strict `PlaceRef`/`RoutePlan` contracts with a new optional `attribution` field, always set to `Google`. Proto3 zero-omission (`distanceMeters`) is decoded faithfully; `duration: "900s"` strings are parsed; a missing route is a typed `MAPS_NOT_FOUND`.
- **Quotas and costs are explicit.** Optional `maxRequests` budget throws `MAPS_BUDGET_EXHAUSTED` (new typed code) *before* dispatch — budget failure is loud, never a degrade. `usage()` reports per-billed-operation counts, cache hits, and remaining budget.
- **Caching within Google policy.** Place-detail cache TTL is capped at 30 days (default 24h), entry-bounded, and concurrent detail reads for one place ID coalesce into a single upstream call.
- **Shared transport extraction.** The bounded SSRF-guarded read machinery (deadline, teardown, retry-after, byte-limited UTF-8 reads) moved from `JsonMapsHttpAdapter` into `src/transport.ts` so both adapters classify untrusted bytes identically instead of duplicating ~200 lines.

## Tests

`plugins/plugin-maps/test/google-adapter.test.ts` drives the **real adapter** against the repository's protocol-faithful fake provider (`@elizaos/cloud-test-mocks/provider-contract`) in both modes, including the full `outbound-http` conformance profile (14 scenarios): success, designed-empty, invalid input, pagination (pageToken forwarding + pageSize clamp), 429 retry-after metadata, malformed JSON, schema drift, timeout, connection reset, 4xx/5xx, opaque connection id, secret redaction, read policy — plus expired vs revoked auth (Google `UNAUTHENTICATED`/`PERMISSION_DENIED` and gateway `credential_expired`/`credential_revoked`), route not-found, coordinate waypoints, cache/coalescing concurrency, budget exhaustion (no request dispatched), managed-path routing and header separation (bearer + connection id, never an API key), constructor SSRF/endpoint validation, and end-to-end `MapsService` registration.


`bun run --cwd plugins/plugin-maps typecheck` and `lint:check` pass; `bun run --cwd plugins/plugin-maps build` passes. # Risks

Medium. This moves Cloud-character quota admission into the primary writer transaction and requires every creation caller to choose a named policy. A mistake could reject or over-admit character creation, or leave the character/mirror split. The implementation therefore locks the organization, serializes the globally unique username claim, counts the exact `source = 'cloud'` population, and inserts `user_characters` plus the required `agents` mirror in one transaction. Lock waits are bounded.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

- Adds mandatory `metered`, `bootstrap`, and `trusted` creation policies with no implicit uncapped default.
- Makes metered creation authoritative on the primary database under one `READ COMMITTED` transaction: organization row lock → billing/settings snapshot → exact Cloud-character count → global username advisory lock → username recheck/scan → character + mirror inserts.
- Returns a typed `CloudCharacterQuotaExceededError` and maps front doors to the canonical quota response.
- Routes `/v1/app/agents`, legacy create, and clone through `metered`; Steward default repair through `bootstrap`; affiliate and trusted S2S through explicit `trusted` policy.
- Removes the stale replica preflight from `/v1/app/agents`.
- Keeps the healthy default-character auth path read-only and repairs only missing/incoherent character/mirror state.
- Adds real PGlite quota/rollback/concurrency tests plus discriminating SQL-order traces.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix: non-breaking enforcement hardening at an existing quota boundary.

# Documentation changes needed?

No public documentation change. Tier values and billing policy are unchanged; this PR makes the already-published character limit enforceable atomically.

<!-- eliza-computer-attribution:v1 {"provider":"openai","model":"gpt-5","client":"Codex","skill_revision":"elizaOS/eliza@a61d938a9e48d437c4a92570bdb028330ff49eaf:packages/skills/skills/contribute-to-eliza"} -->47:T10a2,Fixes #19885

## What

Derives — rather than predesigns — the reusable managed-provider adapter SDK from the existing plugin-maps JSON adapter, and lands it in `@elizaos/core` under `src/integrations/managed-provider/` (exported from the root Node surface):

- **Connection resolver** (`connection.ts`): explicit `managed` (opaque `conn_*` Cloud connection id + gateway origin, no credential ever present at the runtime) vs `local`/BYO (explicit endpoint + user credential) modes. Strict validation of provider ids, opaque connection ids, and origin-only endpoints; **no silent fallback** between modes. Local connections get a deterministic opaque audit handle that never embeds the endpoint or credential.
- **Typed HTTP client** (`http-client.ts`): SSRF-guarded (core `fetchWithSsrfGuard`, DNS-pinned, redirects rejected), origin-pinned, deadline-bounded, byte-limited JSON transport with schema-validated decoding (structural `safeParse` seam, no zod dependency in core) and a designed-null 404 path (`requestOptionalJson`).
- **Failure taxonomy** (`errors.ts`): `ManagedProviderError` with stable codes (auth expired/revoked, rate limited + `retryAfterMs`, timeout, network, endpoint blocked, oversize, malformed, rejected/failure, pagination overflow) and `toCapabilityExecutionErrorCode` mapping onto the #19878 capability execution contract.
- **Pagination** (`pagination.ts`): `collectProviderPages` with page/item ceilings and repeated/empty-cursor rejection so a hostile upstream cannot loop the runtime.
- **Health** (`health.ts`): non-throwing `probeProviderHealth` producing typed snapshots (`healthy` / `reauth_required` / `revoked` / `rate_limited` / `unreachable` / `degraded`) for catalog/kill-switch and Connections surfaces (#19887/#19884).

## Reference migration

`plugins/plugin-maps/src/adapter.ts` is rewritten on top of the SDK (−580/+120 lines of transport code): the SDK owns transport, deadlines, bounded reads, and classification; the plugin keeps the maps route contract, provider-spoofing assertions, and translates the shared taxonomy into `MapsError` codes at its boundary. Managed mode (no credential) and local/BYO mode (credential) both go through `resolveProviderConnection`. The plugin's full existing suite — including `test/provider-contract.test.ts`, which drives the real HTTP adapter against the #19909 protocol-faithful fake upstream — passes unchanged, proving behavior parity. Local mode has no runtime Cloud dependency; the SDK lives in `@elizaos/core` (ESM, project references).

49:Te70,Fixes #19894

## What

Adds a provider-neutral normalized finance capability layer to `@elizaos/plugin-finances`, with freshness/calculation metadata on reads and receipts on internal writes. Read/derive only — nothing implies payments or trading.

**New module `src/finance-capabilities.ts`** (pure, deterministic, no I/O):
- `computeSourceBalances` — per-source ledger-derived net flow (credits minus settled debits). Pending rows are excluded from settled figures and reported separately; every observed currency is surfaced instead of silently merged; metadata explicitly states balances are transaction-derived, not institution-reported.
- `computeBudgetStatus` — user-supplied budget vs settled debit spend in a rolling window (`under_budget` / `near_limit` / `over_budget`), with pending/credit/out-of-window rows excluded and counted.
- `detectAnomalies` — possible duplicate charges (same source/merchant/amount within 3 days) and amount spikes (>= 2.5x a merchant's average over 3+ prior charges, >= $20 delta). Uses the normalized merchant name so adversarial raw strings ("NETFLlX.COM*8873") group with their real merchant; pending rows never generate anomalies.
- `normalizeSubscriptions` — regular-cadence recurring charges (confidence >= 0.5) projected into provider-neutral subscription records.
- `buildCapabilityMeta` — `FinanceCapabilityMeta` on every capability response: `generatedAt`, freshness (`latestDataAt` of consumed rows, counts), and calculation provenance (`derived_from_transactions` | `provider_reported` | `user_supplied_input`, window, notes).
- `buildWriteReceipt` — `FinanceWriteReceipt` (id, capability, operation, entity, occurredAt, outcome, counts) with no credentials or row payloads.

**Action surface (`src/actions/finances.ts`)**: four new OWNER_FINANCES subactions — `balances`, `budget_status` (requires `budgetUsd`, rejects missing/non-positive), `subscriptions`, `anomalies` — and receipts attached to the existing internal writes (`add_source`, `remove_source`, `import_csv`; the CSV receipt is only issued when state actually changed). All exported from the package barrel; docs (CLAUDE.md/AGENTS.md/README) updated.

## Tests

- `src/finance-capabilities.test.ts` — 17 deterministic unit tests over the pure layer: multi-account, multi-currency, pending rows, duplicate/near-duplicate/cross-source negatives, adversarial merchant strings, thin-history spike suppression, empty ledgers, receipt shape/uniqueness, strict pending-flag typing.
- `test/finances.real-db.test.ts` — 4 new real-PGLite integration tests driving `runPaymentsHandler` end to end: write receipts on add/remove source, derived balances + freshness metadata from real rows, budget_status invalid-input + over-budget evaluation, and a real duplicate-charge anomaly plus designed-empty subscriptions.

Results: `bun run --cwd plugins/plugin-finances test` — 12 files, 118 tests passed. Package lint clean; package typecheck clean for plugin-finances sources (the only remaining tsc errors are pre-existing on origin/develop in `packages/ui/src/api/client-cloud.ts` — duplicate `desktopHttpTransportForUrl`/`fetchAgentTransport` imports — untouched by this PR).

## Evidence / notes

- The real path exercised is the PGLite-backed runtime with the plugin's actual `app_finances` schema migration; capability numbers were asserted against inserted rows (e.g. derived net flow 250 with 1 pending row excluded, over-budget remaining -20, duplicate pair `txn-dupe-0`/`txn-dupe-1`).
- No UI change; app visual audit N/A.
- No credentials appear in fixtures, receipts, metadata, or logs.

4a:T14f5,Refs #19883

## What this does

Projects all managed connections into the shared connected-capability API without merging their storage tables, on top of the provider-neutral `ConnectedAccount` contract merged for #19878 (PR #22643).

- **Projection service** (`packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/services/connected-capabilities/`): maps four storage families — cloud OAuth credentials (`platform_credentials`), vendor OAuth connections (`vendor_connections`), Discord connector accounts (`discord_connections`), and native phone-gateway devices (`phone_gateway_devices`) — into contract-normalized `ConnectedAccount` DTOs via the real `normalizeConnectedAccount` from `@elizaos/core`. Storage tables stay separate; the DB loader reads each with an org-scoped SQL filter.
- **Opaque handles, no secrets**: account IDs are `ca_<sha256-derived>` digests of source family + row ID — never credential row IDs. The projection never reads token/ciphertext/DEK/nonce columns; tests assert the serialized payload contains no secret material, email, phone number, or row UUID.
- **Status / last-used / capabilities**: credential lifecycle states map onto contract statuses (`connected`, `reauth_required`, `revoked`, `error`, `disabled`, `unavailable`); granted OAuth scopes project to per-capability risk levels (mutating scopes R2, reads R1); phone-gateway native permission booleans project as capabilities, with ungranted permissions surfaced as `unsupported` rather than hidden.
- **API**: `GET /api/v1/connections/accounts` (offset pagination via the shared fail-closed parser, `providerId`/`mode` filters) and `GET /api/v1/connections/accounts/:accountId` (detail; cross-org and unknown handles 404, malformed handles 400). Mounted ahead of the legacy `/connections/:platform` param route.
- **Worker bundle**: the Workers `@elizaos/core` shim re-exports the pure provider-integration contract pieces from real core source so the Worker runs the actual normalizer, not a stub.
- **Cloud SDK**: regenerated public-route surface adds `getApiV1ConnectionsAccounts` / `getApiV1ConnectionsAccountsByAccountId`, plus mirrored `ConnectedAccount*Dto` types.

## Scope notes

- Local/self-hosted BYO-credential runtimes have no cloud-persisted connection state today; the contract's `local` mode is already served by the enum and the projection's source-loader seam, so a local source registers without an API change when one lands (per the #19877 architecture, local mode never silently falls back to Cloud).
- Rate limiting rides the API-wide middleware; the routes add no per-route override.

## Tests and gates

- `packages/cloud/shared` — 13 new deterministic contract tests over the real projection/service (success, designed-empty, revoked/error/reauth, malformed upstream scopes, soft deletes, pagination totals, cross-org handle isolation, handle stability, fail-closed loader): **13 pass**.
- `packages/cloud/api` — 10 new Hono route tests running the live handlers against a real `ConnectedCapabilitiesService` over typed rows (defaults, pagination, invalid limit/offset/provider/mode, provider filter, empty org, 401, structured 500 without storage context, detail 200/404/400): **10 pass**.
- `packages/cloud/sdk` — full package tests: 195 pass; typecheck clean.
- `bun run verify:cloud` (cloud lint + typecheck lanes, incl. the Worker bundle check): **passing** (83/83 tasks).
- Root `bun run verify` run at head.

Closes #22575.

## What changed

- Registers `workflow_run_event` once per `AgentRuntime` in the canonical agent trigger worker, so standalone and app hosts use the same persisted trigger engine.
- Removes the duplicate app-core Smithers subscription while retaining its connector-event bridge.
- Lists trigger tasks with `agentIds: [runtime.agentId]`, re-reads each queued task before execution, and forwards the native Smithers event without runtime/function objects.
- Serializes events per saved trigger so concurrent emissions preserve current enabled/filter/run-count/max-run state.
- Derives a durable `WORKFLOW_DISPATCH` idempotency key from the saved trigger id plus Smithers native event id. Replays collapse; distinct triggers and distinct native events remain independent.
- Starts trigger processing without awaiting it from the Smithers emitter. Lookup and per-trigger failures go through `runtime.reportError`; one failed target neither rejects the source run nor blocks siblings.
- Extends the real local browser journey to create source/target Smithers workflows, save an exact-step trigger, run the source through the native service, and prove one target trigger execution plus `runCount === 1` after reload.

No second scheduler, HTTP loopback, Gateway protocol, or prose-based routing is introduced.

## Review correction

The predecessor head cached task snapshots for 500 ms and applied a one-second trigger throttle while awaiting the event handler. That could execute a just-disabled trigger, suppress distinct legitimate Smithers events, and delay the source run. Exact head replaces wall-clock admission with native event identity plus canonical dispatch idempotency and fresh serialized task state.

## Exact-head validation

Exact head: `8babb894f38c3d76f15d88d2c545cb6eb97f3cf5` (tree `5276592aee79aff83af942bb4ebafff61bbad15e`)

It created source/target workflows, saved an exact-step trigger, observed one target execution in trigger mode with `runCount === 1`, ran manually, reloaded, and recovered source/widget state.
- Agent trigger runtime suite: 40/40 passed at exact head, including fresh discovery, concurrent state refresh, disable/delete rechecks, runtime isolation, replay idempotency, and distinct rapid events.
- App-core trigger bridge suite: 12/12 passed at exact head.
- plugin-workflow isolated suite: 16/16 files passed at exact head.
- plugin-workflow typecheck: passed.
- Exact-head changed-file Biome and `git diff --check`: passed.
- Agent/app-core/app package typechecks were attempted and remain blocked by missing optional/native workspace build artifacts outside this patch (Capacitor and optional plugin exports); no diagnostic names a changed implementation file.
- Exact-head validation receipt: https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/releases/download/pr-evidence-9/22790-exact-head-validation.md

## Risks

Medium. This is a production event boundary that can start workflows. The native event-id key and per-task queue deliberately replace lossy time throttling; exact native browser evidence and independent exact-head review remain required before merge.

- keeps production Cloud execution disabled by default for manual staging proof (run_cloud_production: false);

## Summary

Implements the server/runtime-side owner-activation contract in `@elizaos/plugin-personal-assistant`:

- **`src/lifeops/first-run/activation.ts`** — `OwnerActivationService`: persists exactly ONE durable assistant activation turn per `owner+agent+contractVersion`. The turn's memory id is deterministic (`stringToUuid` of the activation key), so the durable write itself is the exactly-once anchor: retries, restarts, and a crash between the conversation write and the completion marker all reconcile to the same single turn (`already_complete`) instead of duplicating. Concurrent calls in one process share a single in-flight write.
- **Established-owner negative controls** — an owner with an existing `OwnerFactStore.primaryGoal`, real conversation history in the target room, or a prior contract-version activation is recorded `exempt` (with a typed reason) and receives no canned greeting, preserving #15149's silent entry. Bumping `OWNER_ACTIVATION_CONTRACT_VERSION` never re-activates silently; reactivation requires the explicit `reactivate: true` input.
- **Failure semantics** — a failed durable write throws `ElizaError` (`OWNER_ACTIVATION_WRITE_FAILED`, J2 wrap) and activation is NEVER marked complete; the HTTP boundary translates it to a retryable 503 (J1) so callers retry observably.
- **Routes** (`src/routes/first-run-activation-routes.ts`, wired through the existing OWNER/ADMIN gate in `routes/plugin.ts`): `POST /api/lifeops/first-run/activate` and `GET /api/lifeops/first-run/activation`. This is the server boundary the app onboarding flow calls when handing the owner to the live conversation — not a client check-then-write.
- **First LifeOps goal** — reuses the existing `ftu_goal_discovery` path rather than adding a goal store: the activation turn is goal-forward ("what's the one thing you most want my help with?"), and the evaluator persists the owner's answer into `OwnerFactStore.primaryGoal` exactly once.

## What

Advances the two dispatcher-side residuals the merged bounded-authority slice (#22247) left open on the tracker: **per-domain policy hooks** and **submit interception**.

- **`plugins/plugin-browser/src/browser-domain-policy.ts` (new)** — pluggable `BrowserDomainPolicy` registry on a `globalThis` symbol (same pattern as `browser-capture-hooks.ts`) so both the `BrowserService` dispatcher and the module-level workspace form path consult one source of truth. Evaluation is fail-closed: a throwing policy blocks, an unrecognized verdict blocks, the first non-allow decision wins, and with no registered policies behavior is unchanged. Includes a subaction side-effect classifier (unknown subactions classify as mutating, never read) and a built-in `createBrowserDomainAllowlistPolicy` with `block` / `require_confirmation` verdicts that fails closed on unresolvable domains and rejects suffix-spoofed hosts (`notexample.com` does not match `example.com`).
- **`browser-service.ts`** — `execute()` evaluates every command, including nested batch steps, against registered policies before target selection; non-allow decisions surface as typed `POLICY_BLOCKED` `BrowserDispatchFailure`s (with a distinct message for `require_confirmation`). Nothing has executed at that point, so blocking is side-effect free. The pre-existing generic eval/upload hard block is untouched and independent of policy registration.
- **`workspace/browser-workspace-forms.ts`** — submit interception at the only point where the resolved form-submit URL is known (`submitWebBrowserWorkspaceForm`), before any bytes leave: a click that turns into a cross-domain form submit re-evaluates policies with effect `submit` against the actual submit-URL domain and fails closed.
- Barrel export + bundle-safety binding in `index.ts`; plugin `CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md` updated (byte-identical, parity gate green).

## Tests

20 new deterministic tests in `src/__tests__/browser-domain-policy.test.ts` exercising the real registry, the real `BrowserService` dispatcher, and the real JSDOM workspace submit path (no mock of the system under test). Adversarial coverage: throwing policies, malformed verdicts, empty/whitespace allowlists, suffix-spoofed domains, non-http(s) URLs, unknown-domain fail-closed submits, nested-batch smuggling, and eval hard-block bypass via an allow-everything policy.

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)50:T1061,Fixes #19886

## What

Adds the Cloud credential broker used by first-party plugins for OAuth token refresh and provider API calls. Plugins reference credentials only by opaque connection ID; the broker resolves, refreshes, and injects the provider credential entirely server-side.

- `packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/services/oauth/credential-broker.ts` — the broker service (`CredentialBroker`, DI-friendly `createCredentialBroker`, production `credentialBroker` wired to `oauthService`), with per-platform audience pins (`BROKER_PLATFORM_POLICIES`: exact provider API hostnames for google/microsoft/linear/notion/github/slack/hubspot/asana/dropbox/airtable/zoom/jira/linkedin/twitter).
- `POST /api/v1/connections/:id/broker` — execute one provider request through the connection's credential.
- `POST /api/v1/connections/:id/refresh` — refresh/revalidate the token; returns metadata only (`refreshed`, `expiresAt`, `scopes`) — never token material.
- Router regenerated (`bun run codegen`, 679 routes mounted).

## Security invariants

- **Opaque IDs only**: raw tokens never appear in responses, errors, or logs (asserted in tests).
- **Strip inbound Eliza auth**: the upstream request is built from scratch from the validated JSON body; inbound Authorization/cookies are never forwarded. Caller-supplied credential-bearing headers (`authorization`, `cookie`, `x-api-key`, `x-eliza-*`, `host`, `x-forwarded-*`, …) are rejected with 400, not silently stripped; remaining headers must pass a generic + per-platform allowlist; CR/LF injection rejected.
- **Audience pinning**: https only, exact-hostname allowlist per platform, no userinfo, no ports; lookalike/subdomain hosts rejected. Platforms without a pin (e.g. twilio/blooio) cannot be brokered.
- **No redirect following**: `redirect: "manual"` so a credentialed request never chases an upstream Location.
- **Org/user binding**: connection must resolve under the caller's organization; a user-owned connection is invisible (404) to other users; org-shared connections remain usable; revoked → 401 reconnect-required; expired/pending/error → 401.
- **Refresh race/idempotency**: refresh goes through `oauthService.getValidToken` (versioned token cache + adapter refresh); repeated refreshes are idempotent and report `refreshed: false` when the cached token is still valid.
- Upstream response headers relayed through an allowlist (no `set-cookie`); binary payloads returned base64; 30s upstream deadline; transport failure becomes a typed error with no header/token egress.

## Tests

`packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/services/oauth/credential-broker.test.ts` — 34 deterministic tests with protocol-faithful fetch stub and injected connection/token lookups (no mock of the system under test): hostile URL/host/header/redirect cases, org/user binding, revoked/expired auth, token-egress redaction on success and failure paths, provider 401 passthrough, oversized body, binary payloads, refresh idempotency.

```
34 pass, 0 fail (bun test src/lib/services/oauth/credential-broker.test.ts)
```

## What

Adds `packages/core/src/types/social-compliance.ts`, a validated social integration capability and compliance registry, exported through the core types barrel alongside the #19878 provider-integration contracts it builds on.

Each `SocialComplianceEntry` records, per provider use case:

- **integration status** — `official | business | bot | experimental | handoff | unsupported`
- **platform app-review state** — `not_required | not_submitted | in_review | approved | rejected | expired`
- **allowed operations** — typed `read | draft | write` entries with risk classifications
- **retention/deletion** — `maxRetentionDays` (or explicit `null`) and deletion-propagation support
- **webhooks** — support plus mandatory delivery deduplication wherever supported
- **review accountability** — named owner and ISO reviewed-at date

## Planner visibility (structural, not advisory)

- `SocialComplianceRegistry.projectPlannerCapabilities(providerId)` emits only visible operations as `ConnectedAccountCapability` values; blocked operations are omitted entirely, so `bindCapabilityRequest` (the #19878 authorization path) cannot bind them — proven by test.
- **Posting starts R3**: write operations carry a hard R3 risk floor, drafts R1; normalization rejects any entry below the floor and `bindCapabilityRequest` rejects downgrades.
- `unsupported`/`handoff` entries cannot register operations at all; `experimental` entries cannot register writes; drafts/writes stay hidden until app review is `approved`/`not_required`.
- Unregistered providers/use cases/operations resolve blocked — nothing defaults open. There is no status permitting scraping, self-botting, or mass engagement.

## First-party baseline

Reviewed baseline for discord/telegram/slack/matrix (bot APIs, review not required, R3 posting), x (business, approved), instagram/whatsapp (business, review not submitted — writes and drafts blocked until approval), imessage (device handoff, zero planner-visible operations).

## Tests

25 deterministic tests in `social-compliance.test.ts` (real contracts, no mocks of the system under test): normalization success/invalid-input/duplicate/version/unknown-field paths, risk-floor enforcement, review-state denial across all non-cleared states, handoff/unsupported blocking, planner projection, `bindCapabilityRequest` interop (bind allowed R3 posting, refuse blocked capability, refuse risk downgrade), and baseline integrity.

## Architecture

# Risks

Low. Single-package (`plugin-whatsapp`), one small state flag plus three narrow checks, no public API/signature change (`start()`/`stop()` keep their existing signatures). The fix only ever causes `start()` to return earlier than before (never later, never differently on the non-stopped path) — a session that is never stopped behaves identically to today, confirmed by the added "still restarts normally when stop() is never called" regression test.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

`WhatsAppPairingSession` (`plugins/plugin-whatsapp/src/services/whatsapp-pairing.ts`) manages a Baileys QR-pairing session. On a transient disconnect (`restartRequired`/`timedOut`/`connectionClosed`/`connectionReplaced`), its `connection.update` handler schedules a restart: `this.restartTimer = setTimeout(() => { this.restartTimer = null; this.start()... }, 3000)`. `stop()` only cleared `restartTimer` if it hadn't fired yet — it tracked no "was this session stopped" state at all. If `stop()` runs *after* the timer fired but *while* the resulting `start()` call is still mid-flight (awaiting `useMultiFileAuthState`/`fetchLatestBaileysVersion`, neither cancellable), `start()` had no way to know it should abort, and still ran `this.socket = makeWASocket(...)` at the end — resurrecting a live WhatsApp WebSocket connection for a session the caller believes is stopped.

This is reachable from a public route: `POST /api/whatsapp/pair/stop` and `POST /api/whatsapp/disconnect` (`plugins/plugin-whatsapp/src/api/whatsapp-routes.ts`) both call `session.stop()` and then delete the session from their tracking `Map` in the same request — so the caller has no remaining reference to the resurrected session and can never stop it again short of restarting the process. The resurrected socket keeps firing real `connection.update` events into the original `onEvent` closure, which can still transition to `"connected"` — silently re-establishing a WhatsApp pairing after the user explicitly disconnected it.

The sibling class `BaileysConnection` (`plugins/plugin-whatsapp/src/baileys/connection.ts`) already solves this exact shape of race with socket-identity checks (`if (this.socket !== socket) return;`) in every event handler, with its own pinned regression test (`connection.test.ts`, `"ignores a delayed close event from a replaced socket"`). `WhatsAppPairingSession` never received the equivalent treatment. Socket-identity comparison doesn't directly apply here (the race is "stopped while no new socket exists yet", not "stale event from a replaced socket"), so this PR adds a `stopped` boolean instead: set `true` in `stop()`; reset to `false` at the top of `start()` (so a legitimate re-pair still works); checked in two places — right before the socket is created (aborts an in-flight restart, matching the proven repro) and at the top of the `connection.update` handler (so a stale socket's late events, e.g. a final close event Baileys might still emit after `.end()`, can't schedule yet another restart once the session is stopped).

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fixes (non-breaking change which fixes an issue)

# Documentation changes needed?

My changes do not require a change to the project documentation.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

`plugins/plugin-whatsapp/src/services/whatsapp-pairing.test.ts` (new file) — two tests, mirroring the mocking style already established in `baileys/connection.test.ts`:

1. `"does not resurrect a socket when stop() runs during an in-flight restart"` — pins the exact repro: start a session, trigger a transient close (schedules a 3s restart), advance fake timers to fire it, gate the restart's `fetchLatestBaileysVersion` call open so `start()` is provably mid-flight, call `stop()` while blocked there, then release the gate. Asserts `makeWASocket` is called exactly once (no resurrection).
2. `"still restarts normally when stop() is never called"` — the same transient-close-and-restart flow with no `stop()` call, asserting the restart still happens (`makeWASocket` called twice) — proves the fix doesn't break the legitimate reconnect path it's built on top of.

Confirmed test 1 is a real regression test, not vacuous: reverted the source fix locally and re-ran — it fails with `expected "vi.fn()" to be called 1 times, but got 2 times` (a second socket was created), exactly reproducing the bug. Re-applied the fix and it passes again.

57:Tc53,Fixes #19708

## Root cause (proved in the issue thread)
Runner `eliza-robot-20` on `eliza-staging-robot-1` had **two concurrent `Runner.Listener` processes** (started 2026-07-31 and 2026-08-15) both cwd'd in `/opt/actions-runners/runner-20` and both in the `actions-runner@20.service` cgroup. The hand-provisioned `/etc/systemd/system/actions-runner@.service` uses `KillMode=process`, so a unit restart abandons the previous listener; the two listeners then collide on the slot's `_diag/pages` directory and every routed job dies before checkout. The farm had no repository-owned systemd source of truth (`RUNNER-PLACEMENT.md` explicitly noted this gap), so the defect could recur on any robot host.

## What this PR does
- **`packages/cloud/infra/cloud/runners/actions-runner@.service`** — canonical template unit for the general `eliza-robot-*` farm: `KillMode=control-group` (the load-bearing fix — stop/restart reaps the whole listener/worker cgroup), `KillSignal=SIGINT` + `TimeoutStopSec=5min` for graceful job drain.
- **`packages/cloud/infra/cloud/runners/repair-runner-slot.sh`** — the exact single-slot repair the issue's operator handoff prescribes, as a reviewed, dry-run-by-default script: stop the slot's unit, TERM-and-await abandoned listener chains scoped by `/proc/<pid>/cwd`, preserve `_diag/pages` to a timestamped `pages.issue-19708-<UTC>` sibling (never delete), back up and replace the old unit fragment, daemon-reload, restart only that slot, and fail unless exactly one listener owns it. It never touches sibling slots, runner labels, or `HETZNER_FLEET_ONLINE`.
- **`packages/cloud/infra/cloud/runners/README.md`** — operator flow including the two-verification-job gate before restoring `hetzner-robot`.
- **`packages/cloud/infra/tests/runner-farm-static.test.ts`** — static invariants pinning `KillMode=control-group` (and its absence-of-`process`) in both the new unit and the existing prod-ops cloud-init unit, script `bash -n` validity, executable bit, fail-fast, dry-run gating, diagnostic preservation, single-listener verification, and label/fleet-gate non-mutation.
- `RUNNER-PLACEMENT.md` and the package guide (`CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md`, byte-identical) now point at these assets.

## Human-only remainder
Applying the repair requires root on `eliza-staging-robot-1` (the prior automation lane's `sudo -n systemctl stop` failed on password requirement). An operator runs `repair-runner-slot.sh 20` (dry run) then `--apply`, routes two pinned checkout + workspace-setup verification jobs to runner 20, and only then restores `hetzner-robot` per the fleet owner's broader recovery decision. `HETZNER_FLEET_ONLINE` stays independently managed.

## What

The semantic-stage envelope (`TrajectorySemanticStageRecord`) already existed end to end on the read side — DB step parsing, read-routes projection (`semanticStages` in the detail API), JSONL export, and UI types — but **no production writer ever emitted one**: `recordedStageToSemanticStage` had zero runtime callers. App-chat database trajectories therefore still carried only flat `llmCalls`/`providerAccesses`, while the Stage-1/planner/tool/toolSearch/evaluation decisions lived only in the per-turn JSON file recorder. This PR closes that producer gap without inventing a second stage vocabulary:

- **`packages/core/src/features/trajectories/TrajectoriesService.ts`** — new `logSemanticStage({stepId, stage})`: converts the runtime `RecordedStage` through the shared validated envelope and persists it onto the step's `semanticStages` under the trajectory write lock. Idempotent per `stageId`, capped at the shared 250-stage bound, late captures rejected, invalid stages and detached write failures reported under J7 (`warn` + `runtime.reportError`), never touching the turn. `TrajectoryStep` now carries `semanticStages` with strict round-trip decoding (a present-but-invalid envelope rejects the step instead of decoding to a fake-valid default), and persistence bypasses the sanitizer budget since stages are pre-bounded by the envelope validator.
- **`packages/core/src/runtime/trajectory-semantic-stage-sink.ts`** (new) + **`packages/core/src/services/message.ts`** — the message service wraps its file recorder with `withSemanticStageFanOut` at the single recorder construction point. Every existing `recordStage` emit site (messageHandler, toolSearch, planner, tool, evaluation, subPlanner, compaction, factsAndRelationships) now also lands in the turn's DB step keyed by the ALS `trajectoryStepId`. Fan-out failures are J7 diagnostics and never break the file write or the user turn.
- **`packages/agent/src/runtime/trajectory-storage.ts`** — the capture bridge patches `logSemanticStage` beside `logLlmCall`/`logProviderAccess` (installed patch and `DatabaseTrajectoryLogger` class), persisting stages onto bridge-owned step rows with the same CAS conflict-retry, late-capture, and invalid-capture (J7) semantics. The agent-side persisted step shape, `normalizeStepForPersistence`, and CQRS step rows already round-trip `semanticStages`.

Because the stages reuse `RecordedStage` kinds/payloads verbatim, app-chat DB trajectories, the file recorder, scenario-runner, and native JSONL export now share parity by construction, and the read-routes `semanticStages` projection the Trajectories view consumes is populated for real turns.

## Verification

- `packages/core`: `vitest run src/features/trajectories/TrajectoriesService.test.ts src/runtime/trajectory-semantic-stage-sink.test.ts src/services/trajectory-semantic-stage.test.ts src/features/trajectories/read-routes.test.ts` — **all pass** (new coverage: stage persistence + per-stageId idempotence + strict row round-trip; invalid stage reported under J7 with zero writes; ALS-keyed fan-out; fan-out skip without step id/service; J7 warn+reportError on mirror failure with the file write intact).
- `packages/core`: `tsc --noEmit` — clean.
- `packages/agent`: `vitest run src/runtime/trajectory-bridge.test.ts src/runtime/trajectory-steps.test.ts` — **all pass** (new coverage: patched `logSemanticStage` persists the stage envelope through the agent SQL contract; malformed stage rejected as invalid capture with no write and `TrajectoryStorage.captureValidation` reported).
- `packages/agent`: `tsc --noEmit` — no errors in touched code (remaining TS2307s in this isolated worktree are missing third-party plugin deps — `viem`, `lucide-react` — absent from the worktree's node_modules, unrelated to this change).

## What

Adds a shared capability presentation vocabulary so the Connections (connector account) surface and the Permissions settings surface render connection/grant state through one model, and gives connector accounts the capability/scope UI the issue calls for:

- **`packages/ui/src/components/capabilities/connected-capability-presentation.ts`** — pure, tested model:
  - `readConnectorAccountCapabilityAccess`: sanitizing extractor of granted capability ids from untrusted wire metadata (`grantedCapabilities` / `grantedScopes` / `capabilities` / `scopes` / `requestedCapabilities`); a record reporting no grant info is explicitly `reported: false`, never a healthy-looking empty list (no healthy defaults).
  - `presentConnectorCapabilityChips`: chips built from the provider-declared least-privilege catalog (`CONNECTOR_ACCOUNT_CATALOG`) × reported access — granted, missing-with-Grant, and granted-but-undeclared ids stay visible.
  - `presentConnectorAccountStatus`: exhaustive status → tone/needs-reconnect mapping using the same tone union as the Permissions badges (`StatusBadge` tones).
  - `incrementalScopeRequest`: scope union (granted ∪ requested) so an incremental grant never narrows existing access; unreported access falls back to the full declared set.
- **`ConnectedCapabilityChips.tsx`** — shared chip strip with three distinct states: chips, designed-empty ("No capabilities granted"), and the unreported state ("Access not reported… Reconnect to refresh").
- **`ConnectorAccountCard`** — renders the chip strip (when the provider declares an OAuth capability catalog), a per-capability **Grant** affordance, and a **Reconnect** button for reauth-required/error/disconnected accounts; status badge tone now comes from the shared vocabulary. Purpose/privacy/last-sync display remain.
- **`ConnectorAccountList`** — wires Grant/Reconnect to the existing per-account OAuth restart (`startOAuth` with `accountId` + explicit scopes + requestedCapabilities metadata), with per-flow busy keys and the existing safe-URL open path.
- **`permission-types.ts`** — Permissions badge tone typed onto the shared `CapabilityTone`, unifying the two surfaces' vocabulary.
- **Drive-by:** dedupes a pre-existing duplicate-import typecheck break in `packages/ui/src/api/client-cloud.ts` (develop was red on `tsc --noEmit`).

## Scope notes

The shared connected-capability projection API is sibling issue #19883 (open); per coordination this PR builds only on what exists on develop — the wave-0 contracts in `@elizaos/core` (`provider-integrations.ts`) and the shared connector account catalog. When #19883 lands, its `ConnectedAccount` DTOs map into this same presentation model.

- `ConnectorAccountList.incremental-scope.test.tsx` (integration, real card/chips/model, mocked accounts hook): missing-capability Grant → OAuth restart with `scopes: granted ∪ requested` → simulated provider return renders the capability granted with the Grant affordance gone (the issue's return-and-retry path); Reconnect on a needs-reauth account re-requests its granted scopes; unreported access renders the distinct note; healthy accounts show no Reconnect.

# What changed

Repairs the complete Android evidence path exposed by real emulator and local ARM64 runs:

- raises the cold hosted-bundle timeout from 90 to 180 minutes;
- invalidates stale CMake caches that retained the host `ar`/`ranlib` instead of Zig cross-archivers;
- makes Gradle discover the compiler's documented ARM64 fused-library output;
- enables the deterministic host's real pairing flow, while seeding a generated bearer only for non-onboarding probes;
- makes the first-run device spec redeem a short-lived pairing code through the rendered UI;
- admits the loopback-only pairing-code route through the central policy so its own trusted-loopback guard can run; and
- navigates route coverage through the public shell event instead of a surface-realm-denied raw history write.

# Risks

Medium. This touches CI duration, Android build discovery, and device-test authentication. Security stays fail-closed: OS deep links never accept bearer tokens, the pairing code remains loopback-only and short-lived, and the remote device receives a revocable machine session.

— [codex-open-issues]
<!-- eliza-computer-attribution:v1 {"provider":"openai","model":"gpt-5.6-sol","client":"Codex desktop","skill_revision":"elizaOS/eliza@6d2d20f26c725bed66c2988a1667f244b2cbc2c4:packages/skills/skills/contribute-to-eliza"} -->5e:T1406,## Problem

`extractFirstSentence` is used by reply/TTS early emit. The original implementation copied and regex-matched the full growing prefix at every punctuation candidate, making a single abbreviation-heavy input quadratic.

The submitted linear helper fixed one call, but the production structured-stream consumer still called `hasFirstSentence(accumulated)` on every delta and therefore rescanned all previous text. On pinned Bun 1.3.14, a real accumulated `"Mr. "` sequence took about 0.23s at 1,000 callbacks, 1.48s at 2,000, 5.67s at 4,000, and 21.1s at 8,000: cumulative O(n²) remained.

## Final change

- Replace prefix-copy/regex matching with one rolling ASCII-word/period state machine. This preserves the prior JavaScript `\w` abbreviation semantics and Unicode whitespace/closer behavior.
- Add an incremental scanner and use it in the actual message-service structured-stream/TTS path, so sentence analysis scans each append-only delta once.
- Latch the first completed sentence even when it is too short for voice; the consumer no longer repeatedly re-extracts a permanently ineligible sentence.
- Preserve abbreviation state across arbitrary chunk boundaries, including `Mr.`/`Dr.` and split `e.g.`/`i.e.` tokens. Every chunk-terminal `.?!` is deferred until continuation, whitespace, closers, or EOF disambiguates it, so split decimals and split closer runs match direct input.
- Carry the structured extractor.s attempt revision through the canonical `StreamChunkCallback`, including `createStreamingContext`. Both `useModel` and the primary dynamic-prompt path serialize and drain async structured callbacks before retries and completion, preventing a late old attempt from overwriting authoritative retry state. A retry—including an equal-length prefix rewrite—resets and replays the scanner instead of combining the new attempt with stale abbreviation state. Legacy producers without revision metadata retain length/suffix reconciliation.
- Keep `extractFirstSentence` and `hasFirstSentence` direct-call results compatible. A deterministic 100,000-input differential corpus spanning ASCII punctuation, Unicode text/quotes/whitespace, closers, and malformed fragments produced zero differences from the pre-change implementation.
- Replace the load-sensitive 200 ms assertion with deterministic large direct and 40,000-delta streaming regressions; the standard test timeout still catches the prior hang without asserting scheduler timing.

- Deterministic direct-call differential corpus: **100,000 inputs, 0 semantic mismatches**.
- Deterministic randomized direct-vs-chunked corpus: **100,000 inputs, 0 boundary mismatches**.
- Regressions cover 40,000 stacked direct abbreviations, 40,000 incremental authoritative deltas, every two-way split and one-code-unit chunks, split title/dotted tokens, split closers, decimal continuation, EOF, Unicode whitespace/punctuation compatibility, retry reset/replay, and an equal-length prefix rewrite through the production message/TTS service.
- Pinned linearity scaling after replacing cumulative string equality with revision metadata: 10k **5.4 ms**, 20k **6.8 ms**, 40k **11.8 ms**, 80k **19.4 ms**, 160k **38.8 ms**. This is supporting evidence only; no timing threshold is committed.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

## What

`DiskStore.write` runs `defaultPrivacyRedactor` over every cached tool output before `JSON.stringify`. On origin/develop the redactor walk had no cycle or depth guard.

A cyclic `entry.output` (or a deep nest) `RangeError`s (`Maximum call stack size exceeded`) on the persist path. That is a complete overflow: the tool already succeeded, the cache write then blows the stack.

This head fail-closes the walk: `WeakSet` + depth 8, sentinel `[REDACTED_BOUNDED]`. Credential/geo redaction is unchanged. Cyclic and deep cases no longer throw; `JSON.stringify` of the redacted value succeeds.

## Proof

- Origin walk of `{ self: <cycle> }` → `RangeError`.
- Overlay `bun test src/runtime/tool-call-cache/cache.test.ts` → **22/22**, including cyclic + deep fail-closed cases.

Occupancy-FREE host `packages/agent/src/runtime/tool-call-cache/redact.ts`. Not leftover-tax. Not a fetch-timeout twin.

Bug fix (non-breaking change which fixes an issue)

61:Tdac,## Summary

- Replace the unsafe fixed 10-minute whole-download timeout with two explicit bounds for multi-gigabyte models: 5 minutes without byte progress and a 24-hour absolute backstop.
- Apply one deadline across request headers, the complete response body, and file output. Android uses a signal-aware `pipeline`; the iOS foreground fallback also makes each file write and final flush abort-aware and waits for writer closure before partial-file cleanup.
- Preserve valid slow downloads by resetting only the idle deadline on headers/body chunks. Keep the native iOS background `URLSession` transport unchanged: its OS-owned task is intentionally long-lived and already has start/status/cancel/resume with bounded host calls.
- Validate staged size where known, atomically rename successful downloads, and remove `.part` files on HTTP, body, file, size, or deadline failure.

## Contract research

- Node `stream/promises.pipeline(..., { signal })` destroys the pipeline streams when aborted: https://nodejs.org/api/stream.html#streampipelinestreams-options
- Node abort composition/reasons: https://nodejs.org/api/globals.html#class-abortsignal
- Fetch abort after headers also aborts response-body consumption: https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/AbortController/abort
- Bun documents abortable `fetch` and Node stream compatibility: https://bun.sh/docs/api/fetch and https://bun.sh/docs/runtime/nodejs-apis

## Exact-head verification

Evidence revision: `b91ca672f69427b2183085c6a98b7431bb9025fd`

- Focused production download, Android gating, deadline, gated writer, and iOS bridge suites on pinned Bun `1.3.14`: **55 passed / 0 failed, 183 assertions**.
- Real loopback HTTP + filesystem integration proves progressive transfer beyond one idle window, no-header timeout, stalled-body timeout, peer-drop cleanup, size mismatch cleanup, and absolute deadline despite continuous bytes.
- Real Node `Writable` regressions deliberately stall `_write` and `_final`; abort destroys the writer, waits for `close`, preserves the deadline reason, and lets the caller cancel the reader/remove the partial.
- A real `_write(callback(error))` regression proves the callback error and subsequent Writable `error` event are contained, the promise rejects, and auto-destroy reaches `close` without an uncaught process error.
- Extracted reader-stall teardown orchestration proves reader cancellation and writer `close` both complete before the partial file is removed.
- Fake-timer lifecycle regression proves both timers are live, progress replaces only the idle timer, and `dispose()` clears both timers deterministically.
- `bun run --cwd plugins/plugin-capacitor-bridge typecheck`: passed.
- Biome on all 9 changed files: passed.
- `git diff --check`: passed.

The 12 focused network/file/timer cases were also repeated three consecutive times on pinned Bun `1.3.14` (36/36) after widening the progressive-transfer timing margin. The host Node remains `23.3.0`, rather than the repository-pinned Node `24.15.0`.

This PR is a bounded measurement prerequisite only. It does **not** claim that #22552 is solved, and it does not invent a production baseline or post-change result.

# What changed

- Carry an opaque UUID logical-turn correlation and bounded attempt ordinal across the existing shared `/stream` retry loop. Dedicated-container requests strip these headers, preserving that CORS contract.
- Record one privacy-safe structured completion event for shared `/bridge` and `/stream` attempts, including only allowlisted surface, RPC method, runtime kind, named outcome, HTTP status, logical-turn availability, and first/retry/unknown attempt classification.
- Parse bridge RPC method from a cloned request before dispatch so early warming responses remain sliceable; arbitrary methods collapse to `other` or `invalid`, and request bodies, user text, credentials, and raw agent IDs are never logged.
- Name the two warming outcomes needed by #22552 while classifying all other failures generically.
- Make the documented hot-set selection actually recent-first using the existing authoritative `shared_runtime_history.updated_at`, which is refreshed on every merge; ties are deterministic by agent ID.

The shared-turn baseline event intentionally uses the production-enabled warning level so both successes and failures are observable for a short-lived natural-sample baseline. A follow-up may remove or downsample it only after #22552 has real before/after distributions.

63:Tf7b,Fixes #19887 (parent #19877)

## What

Adds a typed trust/availability/kill-switch policy for the first-party MCP integration catalog and wires it through every surface that advertises or serves those integrations.

- **New policy module** `packages/cloud/api/src/lib/mcp/integration-catalog.ts`
  - `IntegrationTrust` per catalog entry: publisher, provenance (`first-party` / `operator-proxied` / `community`), auth mode, upstream domains, risk-reviewed capabilities (each read/write + reviewed flag), and review date.
  - `resolveIntegrationAvailability(env, id, endpoint)` derives `available` / `disabled` / `unconfigured` from the exact environment contract the transport gateway executes (`MCP_<PROVIDER>_STREAMABLE_HTTP_URL` for operator-proxied providers; built-in Workers transports for time/weather/crypto; `/api/mcp` for the platform server).
  - `MCP_KILL_SWITCH` env var: comma-separated catalog ids and/or provider slugs, or `all`. Parsing is J3-sanitized — malformed input yields an inert switch, never a partially-armed guess.
  - `plannerVisibleFeatures` hides unreviewed **write** capabilities (and any name without a trust record) from planner-facing feature/tool lists.
- **`GET /api/mcp/registry`** — stops advertising unconfigured integrations (previously `linear`, `notion`, `github`, and the endpoint-less `web-search` were listed even though their transport answers 501). Configured entries gain `availability`, `health`, and `trust`. Kill-switched entries stay listed as `disabled` with features, config template, and endpoint withheld. Entries without a trust record fail closed (withheld, warn-logged).
- **`GET /api/mcp/list`** — same annotation and gating; disabled definitions hide their tool list.
- **`/api/mcps/:provider/:transport` gateway** — answers `503 integration_disabled` for kill-switched providers before serving built-in transports or forwarding upstream. Unconfigured non-builtin providers still answer 501 at the transport (they are just no longer advertised).

## Tests

`packages/cloud/api/__tests__/mcp-integration-catalog.test.ts` (12 tests, deterministic protocol-faithful mocks for auth/community-registry/upstream-forward only — routes and policy are real):

- kill-switch parsing of malformed operator input; id/slug/alias matching; `all`
- availability derivation incl. blank upstream URL and kill-switch precedence
- unreviewed writes hidden from planner-visible capabilities and feature lists
- health derivation (operational / unknown / unavailable)
- registry: unconfigured entries never advertised; configured operator proxy advertised with trust metadata; kill-switched entry listed disabled with connection surface withheld; global kill switch
- list: annotation + tool hiding for disabled entries
- gateway: 503 before serving/forwarding when kill-switched; 501 unconfigured; forward when configured

```
bun test __tests__/mcp-integration-catalog.test.ts   # 12 pass / 0 fail
bun test __tests__/mcp-registry-*.test.ts            # 8 pass / 0 fail (existing)
bun run --cwd packages/cloud/api typecheck           # pass (tsc + worker dry-run bundle)
bun run --cwd packages/cloud/api lint                # pass (biome)
```

Unrelated pre-existing failures in the package's full `bun test __tests__` sweep (stripe queue, messages billing, provisioning gate — PGlite/env-dependent) are untouched by this change; CI lanes are authoritative.

## Notes / remainder

- `MCP_KILL_SWITCH` is an optional operator env var (typed via the `Bindings` string index); it is intentionally absent from `wrangler.toml` defaults — setting it in a deployment env arms it.
- Review dates in the trust records are seeded at this change's review; a human security pass should re-stamp `reviewedAt` (and flip `reviewed` on any newly added write capability) as part of future integration reviews — the hiding mechanism is in place and tested.

64:T1377,Fixes #19901

## What

- New shared contract `packages/shared/src/contracts/native-personal-data.ts`: projects the on-device personal-data bridges (location, contacts, calendar, reminders, health, photos, phone, messages) into the provider-neutral connected-account capability contracts from `@elizaos/core` (#19878), as a `ConnectedAccount` with `mode: "native"` plus a richer per-domain status DTO.
  - Pure and deterministic: permission states in, projection out; caller supplies `generatedAt`.
  - Privacy: `residency` is always `"device"`; the projection is metadata-only, enforced at trust boundaries by `assertNativePersonalDataProjectionMetadataOnly` (closed key sets — any payload field throws `NATIVE_PERSONAL_DATA_PAYLOAD_LEAK`). No silent Cloud upload is possible through this contract.
  - Status mapping: granted/limited → available (limited surfaced at domain level), not-determined → `not_configured`, denied → `needs_scope`, restricted → `needs_admin` or `unsupported` by `restrictedReason`, not-applicable → `unsupported`.
  - Offline never degrades availability (data lives on device); foreground-only bridge operations (photos picker read, phone call dispatch) project `provider_unavailable` from a backgrounded app.
  - Fail fast: missing or duplicate domain permission states throw typed `ElizaError`s — no fabricated empty/healthy state.
  - Risk levels: personal-data reads R1 (health R2), on-device writes R2, outward dispatches (call, send message) R3.
- Agent host consumer: `GET /api/permissions/native-projection` in `packages/agent/src/api/permissions-routes.ts`, behind the existing permissions auth gate, built from the live permissions registry or the persisted permission map, with the metadata-only guard asserted before the response is written.

## Tests

- `packages/shared/src/contracts/native-personal-data.test.ts` — 15 tests: every permission status incl. both restricted reasons, denied/limited, background gating, offline invariance, determinism, missing/duplicate fail-fast, unrelated-id tolerance, risk-table contract, payload-leak guard. Full contracts suite: 648 passed.
- `packages/agent/src/api/permissions-routes.test.ts` — projection from persisted state (granted/denied/restricted mix, fallback-to-unsupported for unprobed domains, closed domain key set) and the no-registry/no-state path (all unsupported, account unavailable). File: 10 passed.
- `packages/shared` tsc clean; biome clean on both packages. Pre-existing unrelated cross-package typecheck errors on develop (`cloud/shared` → `@elizaos/plugin-todos/edge`, `ui/client-cloud.ts` duplicate identifiers) are untouched by this change.

## Human-only remainder

Real-device permission probes (iOS/Android grant/deny/limited flows feeding the same `PermissionState` shapes) are physical-device evidence; the projection consumes exactly the states the existing bridges and desktop shell already push through `PUT /api/permissions/state`, which is covered by the fail-closed validator.

# Risks

Medium. Money mutation routes now fail closed when Redis is missing, cannot be constructed, or throws at request time. A Redis outage will 503 those endpoints (with `Retry-After: 30`) instead of falling open. GET billing/payment-request reads, auth, provisioning, inference Durable Object admission, and cron auto-top-up are unchanged.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Adds one shared `moneyRateLimit` helper that forces `failClosed: true`, `localLease: false`, and no `redisUnavailableFallback`. Wires it onto state-changing money mutations including payment-request create/cancel/expire. GET reads stay on ordinary `rateLimit`. Inventory requires listed mutations to mount the helper, walks money-path markers, and fails if a mutating `getPaymentRequestsService` route is unmarked. Cron auto-top-up stays excluded (#20717). `#22999` overlap is the outer POST wrapper plus the `moneyRateLimit` mock export only.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fixes (non-breaking change which fixes an issue)

# Documentation changes needed?

My changes do not require a change to the project documentation.

1. `money-rate-limit-policy.test.ts` — flags cannot be weakened; Redis miss/ctor/runtime → 503; 429 exhaust; no stale lease; mixed-order headers.
2. `money-rate-limit-inventory.test.ts` — listed mounts; marker discovery; payment-request service mutations cannot stay unmarked.
3. Domain-buy and topup fail-closed tests — 503 before handler/side effects.

66:T1858,Closes #22796

## Problem

`parseActionParams` walks untrusted model `{ params }` JSON with recursive `toActionParameterValue`. No depth, node, or cycle budget.

On `origin/develop` `6c1d0a12c910` (Node 24.15.0):

| Input | Result |
|---|---|
| 8k-deep array nest | RangeError 1.72 ms |
| cyclic `{ self: self }` | RangeError 1.42 ms |
| `value[2] = "x"` via `value.map` | length 3, holes at 0 and 1 |

Product path: planner action-parameter parse for the message loop. JSON.parse of the nest succeeds; the walk then stack-overflows the planner.

Not leftover-tax. Not a twin of `#22785` entity-resolution match unwrap, `#22781` inbox priority-scoring flags, `#22768` household entity-id scan, `#22716` cloneJson, or `#22748` in-memory filter. Distinct host: planner `toActionParameterValue` / `parseActionParams`.

## Change

- Extract `toActionParameterValue` / production `parseActionParams` to `action-parameter-value.ts`.
- Fail closed at depth 32 / 2048 nodes / first cycle (`ACTION_PARAMETER_UNBOUNDED`).
- Descriptor-only reads on the whole `{ params }` graph (action slots and parameter slots); enumerable accessors fail closed without invocation. Sparse holes consume the node budget and keep their positions on accepted inputs.
- One shared walk budget across the parsed params graph.
- Array length/indexes and record keys from own data descriptors; every reflective op fail-closes to the typed error (revoked Proxy included).
- Hostile primitive conversion is budgeted and translated with its original cause. Own `__proto__` keys are inert enumerable data rather than prototype mutation.
- JSON-array string coercion parses inside the J3 boundary, then normalizes the full array once outside it so aggregate-budget failures cannot be swallowed.
- Honest scalars, lists, nested records, within-budget sparse arrays, and explicit-`undefined` slots still parse with develop's positional meaning.

## Security and adversarial behavior

- request-body and JSON-RPC `source`, `channelType`, execution, and authentication fields cannot populate the trusted envelope
- the Durable Object validates the envelope after its JSON boundary and rejects unsupported/invalid channel types
- authenticated Personal Shared user role attestation remains a separate server-owned grant; channel provenance cannot grant it
- lifecycle system turns retain their action-free system source/DM semantics

## Summary

Staging webhook flows 503 `WEBHOOK_GATEWAY_NOT_CONFIGURED` until the gateway is provisioned. Most of the repo-side work from this issue already landed on develop (`deploy-gateway-webhook.yml` staging path with canonical Railway/service/domain validation, the Dockerfile Bun pin, `ELIZA_ONBOARDING_LOGIN_APP_URL = "https://staging.eliza.app"` in `[env.staging.vars]`). What remained codeable was the deploy-time contract on the Worker side: `cloud-cf-release.yml` publishes `ELIZA_APP_WEBHOOK_GATEWAY_URL` as a routing toggle and would silently accept two miswires that turn the honest 503 into misleading failures:

1. **Cross-environment miswire** — staging Worker pointed at the production gateway (or vice versa). Now the URL must byte-match the canonical per-environment origin, the same values `deploy-gateway-webhook.yml` pins as `EXPECTED_GATEWAY_URL` (a test keeps the two in sync).
2. **Unpaired forwarder secret** — a configured URL without an existing-or-queued `ELIZA_APP_WEBHOOK_GATEWAY_SECRET` Worker binding. The gateway's `enforceForwarderSecret` would then 401 every forwarded webhook, strictly worse than the honest 503.

Changes:
- `packages/cloud/scripts/verify-webhook-gateway-binding.mjs` — pure contract check + names-only CLI (stdin = `wrangler secret list` inventory, argv = queued names; secret values never read or printed), mirroring the existing candidate verifiers.
- `.github/workflows/cloud-cf-release.yml` — `verify_webhook_gateway_binding_candidates` runs after the other candidate verifiers and before any Worker mutation; unset URL remains a clean no-op.
- `packages/cloud/scripts/__tests__/verify-webhook-gateway-binding.test.ts` — deterministic tests: honest-unset path, both canonical environments, cross-environment miswire, missing paired secret, queued-name satisfaction, unknown environment, combined failures, inventory parser (bare array + `{result}` wrapper + malformed rejection), and the workflow-wiring/URL-sync contract.

## Verification

- `bun test packages/cloud/scripts/__tests__/verify-webhook-gateway-binding.test.ts` — 12 pass, 0 fail.
- Existing workflow-contract suites still green: `voice-realtime-deploy-contract.test.ts`, `personal-telegram-edge-deploy-contract.test.ts` (5 pass).
- CLI exercised directly: canonical staging URL + secret → exit 0; production URL on staging with empty inventory → both `::error::` lines, exit 1; unset URL → no-op notice, exit 0.
- `bunx biome check` clean on both new files; `actionlint` reports no findings on the added workflow lines.

# Risks

**Low.** One decimal-arithmetic branch swap inside an already-locked transaction; no schema, route, or API-surface change. The funding-order change is user-visible only in *which pool* gets debited (now matching what docs/UI/policy already promised). Worst case for an org with both pools: purchased credits stop being drained before redeemable earnings — the exact intended behavior change. Idempotency, receipt, ledger, and cursor writes are untouched; the concurrent-replay fence is re-proven below.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Makes container settlement honor the earnings-first policy atomically, closing the #22951 contradiction:

- **Policy** (`packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/services/container-billing-policy.ts:78-79`): `fromEarnings = min(earningsEligible, dailyCost)` — toggle on → earnings absorb the charge first, credits cover the remainder ("survival economics").
- **Implementation before** (`packages/cloud/shared/src/db/repositories/container-billing.ts:323-324`): `creditApplied = Decimal.min(creditAvailable, amount); earningsApplied = amount.minus(creditApplied)` — inverted: purchased credits were consumed first and earnings only covered the overflow.

The fix swaps the application order inside the existing locked transaction (row locks + advisory lock unchanged): `earningsApplied = Decimal.min(earningsAvailable, amount); creditApplied = amount.minus(earningsApplied)`. Split, earnings conversion (4dp ROUND_UP), both ledger rows, the credit debit, the receipt/idempotency record, and the billing cursor all still commit in that one transaction.

Decimal semantics are preserved exactly: earningsApplied is capped by the live 4dp earnings row, so `ceil4(earningsApplied) ≤ earningsAvailable` still holds; credits keep any sub-0.0001 conversion change; no unbacked debit and no money creation. Toggle-off behavior is unchanged (earnings row is never locked → `earningsAvailable = 0` → credits-only, earnings never ledgered).

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fixes (non-breaking change which fixes an issue)

# Documentation changes needed?

My changes do not require a change to the project documentation. Policy (`container-billing-policy.ts` JSDoc), the `pay_as_you_go_from_earnings` schema comment (`organizations.ts:82-86`), `packages/docs/cloud/earnings.mdx`, `packages/docs/cloud/containers.mdx`, and the UI copy (`pay-as-you-go-card.tsx`: "Earnings will pay container hosting before credits") **already document earnings-first** — the repository was the only surface contradicting them, so this PR aligns implementation to the published contract (the "align policy, schema comment, docs, UI copy, and implementation" AC).

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

`packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/services/__tests__/container-billing-idempotency.test.ts` → the new `describe("earnings-first settlement order (#22951)")` block at the bottom: six PGlite-backed cases that execute the real settlement SQL (FOR UPDATE locks, JSONB idempotency lookup, partial unique indexes) against seeded mixed pools.

For the concurrent-replay acceptance criterion specifically: `packages/cloud/shared/src/db/repositories/__tests__/container-billing-concurrent-replay.integration.test.ts` → a real-Postgres integration test (ephemeral container, same harness as `compute-stop-concurrency.integration.test.ts`) proving the fence under two GENUINELY overlapping settlement transactions. PGlite is a single session, so the PGlite suite's replay case can only prove the sequential post-commit retry; the integration test proves the in-flight overlap.

```
(pass) concurrent settlement replay (earnings-first) > two overlapping settlements contend on the row lock: one settles, the loser converges on the already-billed fence [62.35ms]
```
